passes that existing typed segment policy to its shared call query. Declaration

only required region/staging consequences change.

The new executed `tests/controls/nested-scalar-choice-temporary-lifetimes.t`
covers then/else/sibling chains, condition temporaries, throwing destructors,
class receiver conversion and tracked nonroot conversion. PA12 owns the
temporary value semantics and conversion operators used here. Its runtime
destruction checks and protected raw-byte comparison pass. All 361 fixed PA12
cases and the full course suite pass: 318/318 main, 135/135 controls; no failed
counts or marker. Remaining PA12 fixed failure IDs: none.

## Nonthrowing segment-floor follow-up from the PA17 checkpoint audit (2026-10-18)

Reviewed implementation: `770cf0cca1e2bc49c1549db4302d627a2f1e7c42`; entry PA17 source:
`54d14a156d391af69356342d2a47a02e9ada0dce`. PA12's stage base and historical
full-stage audit markers above remain unchanged. The PA17 checkpoint audit
found a shared nonthrowing-region defect with an ordinary no-template reduction:
an inert receiver closes the initial region, but Rust reopened an empty one
around the next call when the only automatic objects were below the segment
floor. C++ `cleanup_lowering.cpp::temporary_done/region_event/open_region`
requires an active chain for later openings; only the initial event may be empty.

`lower/lifetime_regions.rs` now tests cleanup depth against the segment floor
at both reopening sites and distinguishes the pending initial event. The new
executed control `tests/controls/inert-receiver-nothrow-segment-floor.t` belongs
here because it uses ordinary return-by-value and temporary materialization.
Its protected Types/Semantics/LowIR comparisons and course execution pass.
The existing 361/361 fixed PA12 cases and the full PA12 course (318/318 ordinary,
134/134 controls) remain passing; there are no remaining PA12 fixed failures. No frozen input or reference changed.

The PA17 ledger's newest audit entry records the wider owner review, typed
ownership, build/quality/audit commands, all prior stages, resource samples and
protected stack qualification. Full evidence is in
`/home/vishvananda/work/private/v5rust/artifacts/pa17-checkpoint-audit-z`:
`inert-receiver-entry.jsonl`, `receiver-probes-final.json`, `prior-final.log`,
`pa12-results.json`, `course-pa12/` and `evidence-audit.json`. The new control's
source is included in `tested-sources-final.json`; no template or backend work
was forced into PA12. Ralph's state and stage advancement remain untouched.

## Previous callable copy/move group and architecture review

Recursive copy properties remain separate from callable plans. A property owns
a pending/in-progress/ready `ConstructorId` memo. An explicit dependency stack
walks only the recorded selected subobject edges, completing each child before
its parent; it does not run overload resolution again or synthesize syntax.

A demanded constructor borrows its canonical class, source signature and
selection facts. Its unit-owned plan holds declaration-ordered
`CopyTarget::{Base(index),Member(EntityId)}` actions with `TypeId` and optional
selected child `ConstructorId`. Implicit callable entries get a public inline
function identity and one positional source parameter; an origin enum selects
`other` in the LowIR prologue. Written defaulted entries retain their original
parameters, declaration identity, exception source and complete/base variants.
A written out-of-class definition publishes its callable plan. Repeated uses
share the memo; unused implicit bodies are not emitted.

Lowering preserves constructor calls versus trivial storage transfers. It
handles recursive base/member copies, move-selected child calls, references
rebound as pointers, scalar arrays and flattened class arrays. The leading run
of trivial subobjects uses one storage prefix. Complete-object and base-entry
limits protect reused tail padding; empty objects/subobjects preserve the C++
evaluation/address rules. Direct local initialization still calls a written
trivial move constructor; delegation to a selected trivial copy instead uses
storage or prvalue materialization. Both copy/move constructor pointer operands
retain noalias/object extents through the existing entry interface.

Constructor and assignment bit-field transfers share one typed storage-view
algorithm. It transfers a safe adjacent allocation unit once, respects
zero-width separators, volatile/overlapping subobjects and the appropriate byte
limit, and retains field-wise operations where a unit transfer is unsafe.
Assignment selection and body ordering are otherwise unchanged.

Class arrays unroll through eight elements and use one target-width prefix
counter above eight. Completed destructible subobjects join an address-owned
plain cleanup chain only where a later copy/default evaluation can throw.
Handlers destroy the completed prefix in reverse order, including flattened
arrays, and guard a throwing destructor with terminate handling. A successful
array replaces its element records with one array action when needed by a later
copy. No array walk uses native recursion.

Default-argument temporaries end with their selected copy call. Ordinary
segments serve the first copy; nested explicit temporary regions serve a call
inside a completed-object chain. Throwing temporary destructors close the
ordinary segment before normal destruction. Scope state is restored after the
call. Caller-owned by-value default arguments use their recorded class passing
facts. The boundary worklist follows demanded constructor identities and
selected nontrivial copy actions, preparing those signature facts exactly once
instead of examining unrelated functions.

The ownership regression verifies unchanged syntax node counts, one reused
constructor memo, public implicit access, an unprepared unused class, an
undemanded move plan and an external leaf body that remains external. All body,
array, temporary and cleanup state belongs to one `Function`; the program keeps
only typed LowIR and entry aliases. No optimizer, allocator or native owner is
changed. No unsafe code, new dependency, environment/process access, reference
recognition, textual phase transport or compiler fallback is introduced.

## Oracle defect: explicitly noexcept defaulted copy

`probes/defaulted-nothrow-guard.cpp` is a standalone reproducer: class `B` holds
`A`, whose user-provided copy can throw; `B(const B&) noexcept = default` is used
by `main`. Frozen C++ exits 0 but emits `eh_try ^function_exception_landing_1`
without defining that block. Its own `lowir -o <out> <dump>` exits **1** with
`undefined or invalid block target`; `oracle-defect-validation.log` and `oracle-defect.json` record the
error, commands and both exit statuses; `oracle-defect/invalid.lowir` retains
the freshly reproduced frozen output. Raw comparison evidence is in
`mismatches/b3b939271fb44902ada0f949c19c7458/output-0.{oracle,candidate}`;
the original C++ early synthesized-copy return is at
`lower/function_lowering.cpp` lines 215–222, before the ordinary guard epilogue.

Rust emits the landing block and terminate helper, so this input does **not**
have byte parity. No oracle, fixture, checker, acceptance rule or reference was
changed. This baseline defect needs an oracle decision; it is not a waiver or a
completed parity claim. Independent copy/move work continued as instructed.

## Current conditional group and architecture review

This group starts from clean commit `226acf087a7949a1852d99f4d00bf1aa3d409f3b`
and 221/361 fixed-manifest passes. The preceding goal turn is **progress**:
its accepted callable-copy implementation and retained 207 → 221 evidence
change the next dependency boundary. Neither stage base nor last-audited
commit is advanced here.

C++ owners read directly are
`sema/analyzer_conversions.cpp::{conditional,converted_conditional,conditional_match}`,
`sema/analyzer_construction.cpp::{copy_initialization,record_copy}`,
`lower/object_lowering.cpp::{initialize_from,materialize_prvalue,temporary_object,initialize_subobjects}`,
`lower/statement_lowering.cpp::initialize_result`,
`lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp::{begin_initializer,temporary_region,region_event,close_region}`,
and the existing typed LowIR writer/definition-order owners.

Rust `sema/class_conditionals.rs` keeps cv/category selection ahead of copy
demand. Same-class and related-base prvalue matches record two original
operand uses, selecting glvalue transfers while leaving branch prvalues in
place. `sema/copy_initialization.rs` owns selected `ConstructorId` or storage
transfer plus explicit outer-copy elision permission. Source type/category/cv
stay on the original syntax. A checked base path belongs to the selected
binding; derived sources preserve their value category, and an ordinary
converting constructor can still beat the base copy. Direct initialization
retains a written defaulted move's callable entry even when its transfer is
trivial. No unrelated class or body is prepared speculatively.

Rust `lower/class_conditionals.rs` builds the selected branch into one
address. `materialization.rs` distinguishes constructing the source prvalue
itself from applying its outer initialization; reference materialization
therefore cannot replay the outer copy recursively. Nontrivial same-class
conditional copy initialization retains private source storage and emits
`[elision=copy]`; direct initialization and slicing retain ordinary calls.
The permission is a typed fact, never inferred from output names.

`lower/class_results.rs` recursively constructs a returned conditional in its
selected branch. The parent's selected glvalue argument binding is shared
exactly as in C++ `initialize_result`; branch-specific conversions are not
recomputed. Branch temporaries, including containing objects whose members
supply the result, are destroyed after the result transfer. Enclosing tracked
temporaries remain alive until their full-expression ends.

`lower/temporary_scan.rs`, `lifetime_regions.rs` and `member_initialization.rs`
share initializer boundaries and inspect selected copy calls in throw scans.
A `RegionStart::{Pending,Consumed}` state distinguishes a pending segment start
from an open region, preserving before/after-address placement, branch pauses
and nonthrowing region events. Explicit element scopes save and restore this
state. A member initializer destroys its expression temporaries before the
completed member joins the constructor unwind chain.

An inert destructor defined before a later local object can acquire
`object_root` after its body has streamed. `lower/definition_order.rs` now keeps
its `SymbolId` and serialized body span while releasing its typed body
immediately; the final header renders from typed program metadata. The shared
LowIR writer has separate header/body renderers. It never reparses or edits
serialized text, and no function IR or semantic storage is retained for this
late metadata dependency.

The semantic ownership regression checks unchanged syntax counts, original
operand facts, constructor memo reuse, cv/category selection, explicit direct
versus copy-init permission and no implicit-copy preparation for an unused
class. The LowIR regression checks body release, preserved defined identity
and a late root observed by the deferred header. All expression/branch/region
state stays per function. No dependency, unsafe code, environment/process read,
optimizer, register allocator or native generator is changed.

## Current supported hot paths and protected stack qualification

`measure.py` generates one shared value class and **100/400/1,600** emitted
functions returning mixed lvalue/prvalue conditionals. All three outputs match
through the protected adapter (`scaling.jsonl`). After all validation processes
finished, an A/A calibration preceded two paired ABBA blocks: four measured
observations per tool/input. Means include startup; peak RSS is the largest
observation. Sources, raw times, streams, output hashes and source sizes remain
in `scaling/`, `resources.json` and `resources-final.log`.

| Conditional bodies | Rust mean ms / peak RSS KiB | C++ mean ms / peak RSS KiB |

| 100 | 24.08 / 8748 | 7.64 / 7988 |
| 400 | 40.08 / 8780 | 13.06 / 9424 |
| 1600 | 107.97 / 18600 | 38.33 / 14972 |

about **2.13x**. Startup dominates small inputs. This is a supported-path
observation, not a universal complexity proof or a PA35 generated-code claim.

guard returns exit 1; the protected adapter's `stack.jsonl` receipt has
**match=true** and qualification **passed=true**. Both tools succeed with equal
streams, deterministic fields and output hashes at a common **16 MiB**. Raw
initial and qualified attempts remain in
`stack-qualifications/71e8809471c44d589cc7cd8f8207ea73/`.
Acceptance reads the qualification receipt because the adapter retains the
original candidate exit/streams. No guard budget, native exhaustion cutoff or
diagnostic position was calibrated or changed.

## Next dependency boundary

Conditional matching between unrelated classes and through conversion functions
is still missing at `conditional unrelated class conversions` and
`conditional class conversion functions`. The next coherent owner is
`sema/analyzer_conversions.cpp::conditional_match/builtin_conditional` and its
conversion candidate machinery. Preserve bidirectional ambiguity, explicit
conversion restrictions, second standard-conversion ranking and access/deletion
checks before adding lowering for those selected conversions.

Other residual owners include array new/cookies/checks/delete; reference and
condition/EH cleanup; general user conversions and builtin fallbacks;
assignment by-value parameter copies and implicit move-assignment selection;
and remaining default-constructor bit-field ordering and scalar literal
conversion. The explicitly-noexcept defaulted-copy oracle defect above remains
reported and unresolved. Full PA12 remains partial; exact residual identities
below and protected artifacts, rather than a fixture count, define its state.

## Independent checkpoint audit — 2026-10-15

Reviewed implementation commit: `33834ebef3f84b9fc8eeb7d40dbec92a91fc44eb`.
The review covers the entire diff from stage base
`b9f3dba87a8cc2859ff489ed09f1ddf2cb95dc4f`, including accepted checkpoints
`c83439e`, `226acf0` and `6106df2`, and the audit fixes. The preceding accepted
turn is **progress**: committed conditional work and protected 221 → 243 evidence
establish the starting checkpoint. This audit certifies the reviewed partial
checkpoint, not the full assignment. The 118 residual case identities listed
above are unchanged and were checked individually against `progress.json`.
The next dependency boundary remains unrelated-class conditional conversion
selection; allocation, general conversion and remaining assignment work is not
required to pass this checkpoint.

Read `AGENTS.md`, `CLONE_CONTRACT.md`, `spec.md`, `pa12/README.md`, the conversion
plan, this ledger, the source map and all changed source responsibilities.
Consulted frozen C++ owners directly: `sema/analyzer_copy.cpp`,
`analyzer_construction.cpp`, `analyzer_conversions.cpp`, `lower/copy_lowering.cpp`,
`object_lowering.cpp`, `cleanup_lowering.cpp`, `tracked_lifetime_lowering.cpp`,
`statement_lowering.cpp`, `function_lowering.cpp`, definition ordering and the
LowIR model/writer. The Rust owners are those recorded above and in the updated
source map; the fixes stay in `lower/{construction,aggregates,materialization,
class_conditionals,lifetime,lifetime_regions,temporary_scan,tracked_lifetimes,
function}.rs` and `sema/layout.rs`.

### Findings and corrections

1. **Nested conditional local construction opened an extra tracked region.**
   Rust retained only a call's insertion position, losing the C++ `CallMark`'s
   declaration origin. `V w = a ? (b ? v : V(2)) : V(3)` and its direct-init
   counterpart emitted an extra `eh_try`/`eh_end` before branching. Call marks
   now retain a typed declaration flag. A named destination identifies its
   outer construction call; tracked earliest-call placement excludes that
   mark, while ordinary call-region placement still sees it. Both forms and
   nested arguments/repeated declarations now match exact C++ bytes.
2. **Ordinary branch copy default temporaries returned status 86.**
   `V w = c ? v : V()` with `V(const V&, const T& = T())` lacked the ordinary
   conditional guard scope supported by C++. Class-conditional materialization
   now owns the outermost flag-reset insertion position; nested branches retain
   its advancing position. A branch temporary sets its flag before closing its
   construction region, survives the outer transfer, and is destroyed only on
   the path where it was made. It does not force whole-expression tracking.
   Normal cleanup preserves the C++ flag-test region and continuation order.
3. **Throwing copy-default temporary destructors had incorrect unwind ownership.**
   Their construction region could cover normal destruction and destroy the
   same temporary again on failure. Temporary destruction now closes that
   region first and uses explicit earlier-object suffixes. A throwing temporary
   destructor after local initialization also owns a cleanup for the completed
   local, above the earlier temporary suffix. These are typed chain records;
   building the handler does not clone or temporarily truncate the cleanup
   vector. Inert local destructors remain governed by the existing C++ rule.
4. **Class-valued copy default arguments lacked demanded destructor identities.**
   A selected `C(const C&, B = B())`, where `B` has destructible members, could
   fail at `implicit recursive destructor lowering`. The reached-signature
   boundary now prepares by-value parameter destruction. Newly published
   containment edges enter the same reached-owner worklist. Temporary scans
   include the selected copy's caller-owned class parameters. Repeated calls
   reuse the facts; an unused signature still emits nothing.

Four new required runtime controls are in PA12's existing executed lane:
`400-audit-nested-conditional-construction.t`,
`400-audit-conditional-copy-default-lifetime.t`,
`400-audit-conditional-copy-throwing-destructor.t` and
`400-audit-copy-class-default-destruction.t`, all under `pa12/tests/controls/`.
They verify selected nested values, branch-dependent temporary counts, normal
execution with potentially throwing destructors, and repeated destruction of
class-valued defaults. Each also has an exact protected compiler invocation
receipt. Frozen inputs, references, checker policy and Ralph state were not
changed.

### Algorithm and ownership review

Delegation keeps canonical constructor identity, selected arguments and the
original initializer. It checks the sole-initializer restriction and traverses
the selected chain before publishing the new delegation edge. Complete/base
cleanup variants remain explicit. A failed semantic/lowering operation aborts
its unit/function owner; it does not publish successful emission or retry
through a textual fallback.

Copy properties and callable plans remain separate memos with explicit
not-started/in-progress/ready states. The dependency stack visits selected
containment edges, not the global function registry. Syntax is retained once;
plans contain compact class/member/type/constructor IDs. Lowering reads the
selected constructor and binding, preserving access/deletion failures,
reference rebinding, move selection, default arguments and demand-only emission.
The existing semantic regressions verify unchanged syntax cardinality, memo
reuse and unused-body isolation.

Copy lowering preserves declaration order, leading storage prefixes, base
nonvirtual byte limits, safe bit-field unit transfers and field-wise fallback.
Array products and byte offsets use deliberate target-width wrapping; loop
indices are target i64 values. Small arrays use the C++ eight-element boundary;
large/multidimensional arrays retain one prefix counter and reverse destruction.
No new numeric representation or diagnostic normalization was introduced.
Independent const/volatile and private-base probes compare ordinary rejection
bytes exactly, in addition to successful output comparisons.

Conditional branches retain original cv/category and selected base routes;
outer copy-elision permission is a semantic fact. Return lowering shares the
parent glvalue binding and destroys branch-containing objects after transfer.
Per-function marks, flags, region state and cleanup chains die with that
function. The late inert-destructor header retains a symbol ID and rendered
output span after releasing its typed body; it never reparses output. Repeated
multi-unit probes verify coalescing, anonymous-class isolation and late roots.
No second parser, unsafe code, C++ delegation, fixture recognition, environment
read, new dependency, blanket lint suppression, mechanical file split, optimizer
or register allocator change was found in the reviewed diff or introduced by
the fixes. Source responsibilities and private typed stores were reviewed
alongside the authoritative file audit.

## PA12 by-value assignment parameter group (implementation handoff)

Group base: `d295c54` (clean entry; preceding independent audit made **no fixed
manifest progress**, 243/361). Stage base and last-audited commit above are
unchanged. This is a coherent partial implementation handoff for Ralph's
checkpoint review, not PA12 advancement or full-stage completion.

### C++ owners, implementation and typed flow

Consulted directly in the frozen oracle:

- `sema/analyzer_copy.cpp::{copy_parameter,subobject_copy,
  defaulted_copy_facts}`: a selected by-value subobject assignment parameter is
  copy-initialized from the source subobject. Explicit constructors are excluded;
  move/copy source category, deletion, access and exception facts are retained.
- `lower/copy_lowering.cpp::{copy_call,copied_parameter,call_copy,copy_elements}`:
  allocate the parameter object, call its selected constructor or transfer its
  storage, end constructor-default temporaries, assign, then destroy the caller's
  parameter on normal and unwind paths. Small arrays unroll through eight;
  larger/multidimensional arrays use the existing flattened target-i64 loop.
- `lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp::{begin_segment,temporary_region,temporary_alive,
  emit_call,end_full_expression}`: segment placement, live-object transitions,
  pointer-result spill order and shared cleanup continuations.
- `sema/analyzer_declarations.cpp::default_definition` and
  `lower/function_lowering.cpp::lower_definition`: a written defaulted
  assignment has a synthesized body and positional unnamed parameter binding,
  including when its declaration/definition names the source parameter.

Rust owners: `sema/{assignment,copy_initialization,copy_properties,layout}.rs`
and `lower/assignment.rs`; shared constructor, array and lifetime algorithms
remain in their existing owners. `docs/port/source-map.tsv` records the changes.

An assignment action retains its selected `EntityId` and copy selection.
The existing reached-function worklist follows only those selected edges and
publishes the parameter's canonical `ConstructorId` when lowering needs it.
Constructor plans reuse the existing memo; parameter layout/passing/destructor
facts use the existing signature boundary traversal. Repeated uses share facts;
unused containing classes remain unprepared. The new semantic regression checks
that transition, selected parameter identity, memo reuse and unchanged expression
and declaration cardinalities. No syntax is synthesized or reparsed.

Lowering consumes the retained constructor, distinguishing calls from storage
transfers and preserving empty-class construction with nontrivial destruction.
Each parameter lives in its function-owned `argobj` slot. Its constructor's
caller-owned defaults end before the assignment; the parameter then becomes
live, the selected assignment call retains its exact pointer spill, and the
parameter is destroyed before the next element/subobject. Throwing destructors
reuse the ordinary lifetime owner's exceptional cleanup logic. The existing
array threshold, source/destination order, bit-field/storage-prefix policy,
nonvirtual spans and target wrapping arithmetic are preserved. All region,
slot, cleanup and loop state dies with the function.

The defaulted parameter-name correction preserves the original rvalue-reference
signature and ABI identity. No optimizer, register allocator, native code,
stack guard, dependency, environment/process read, unsafe code, C++ fallback,
fixture recognition, reference or protected policy was changed. Source
responsibilities and ownership were reviewed alongside strict Clippy and the
independent file audit.

Two new required controls are in PA12's existing executed lane:
`tests/controls/400-assignment-byvalue-array-default-lifetime.t` checks small and
multidimensional arrays, constructor-default lifetime, thirteen parameter copies,
caller destruction and unchanged sources; and
`tests/controls/400-assignment-explicit-parameter-copy-bad.t` checks exclusion of

## PA12 named automatic return group (implementation handoff)

Group base: `737e9e3025faf30889779b09337228e06a6c5aba`, clean entry,
**245/361** fixed-manifest passes. The preceding implementation turn is
**progress**: its committed by-value assignment work and retained 243 → 245
identity evidence establish this base. Stage base and last-audited commit at
this ledger's head remain unchanged. This handoff completes the named-return
selection and resulting special-member ordering group for checkpoint review;
PA12 remains partial and Ralph owns advancement.

### Frozen owners and implementation

Read `AGENTS.md`, `CLONE_CONTRACT.md`, `spec.md`, `pa12/README.md`, the Rust
conversion plan and this ledger, and consulted these frozen C++ owners directly:

- `sema/analyzer_bodies.cpp::{return_statement,names_automatic_object,moves_from}`:
  nonvolatile automatic local/parameter eligibility, parenthesis removal,
  function ownership, reference/static/extern/TLS exclusions and two-pass
  constructor selection.
- `sema/analyzer_calls.cpp::{resolve,best_viable}` and
  `sema/analyzer_construction.cpp::{same_class_source,copy_initialization,record_copy}`:
  viability/ranking precedes access/deletion; an xvalue probe is kept only when
  its first parameter is an rvalue reference to the source object's unqualified
  type. A failed, ambiguous or differently typed probe retries the original
  lvalue. A kept deleted/private move diagnoses rather than retrying a copy.
- `lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp::{creates_temporaries,begin_full_expression,call_may_throw}`,
  `lower/statement_lowering.cpp::class_return`, `lower/copy_lowering.cpp::call_copy`
  and `lower/object_lowering.cpp::call_special`: nonelided initialization
  conversions participate in the temporary query; the hidden return segment
  brackets constructor calls even when noexcept, preserving default-argument
  temporary cleanup and handler ordering.
- `lower/definition_order.cpp::{note_family_members,class_identity,
  order_special_member_families}` and
  `lower/program_lowering.cpp::order_completed_definitions`: constructor and
  assignment forms occupy their existing definition positions, copy before
  move, followed by base-before-complete entry ordering.

Rust owners: `sema/{procedural_statements,copy_initialization,lowering}.rs`
and `lower/{temporary_scan,construction,definition_order,symbols}.rs`.
The source map records each translated responsibility and its typed flow.

### Ownership, demand and source review

Return eligibility consumes the original resolved `BindingId`, declared type,
automatic-storage facts and enclosing function `ScopeId`; it performs no lookup.
Parenthesized expressions share their existing facts. A compact
`CopyResolution::{Selected,NoViable,Ambiguous}` separates expected resolution
outcomes from diagnostics. The xvalue view is a small copied fact record,
not a syntax node. The kept pass reuses its `CopySelection`, including the
selected canonical reference binding; discarded probes prepare no callable
constructor plan or emission demand. Ordinary copy uses retain their existing
access/deletion diagnostics. Published operand type, category and independent
conversion remain intact. Constructor-property memos and cleared resolution
scratch are reused through the existing owner.

Lowering reads that selected action. Counting a nontrivial transfer result in
the temporary query does not register the returned object as a caller-owned
temporary. Its default arguments use the existing per-function cleanup owner,
with construction, transfer, destruction and exceptional cleanup in C++ order.
The hidden segment retains noexcept calls' prescribed regions; it introduces
no optimization or new language exception policy.

Read-only `CopyForm` facts retain the canonical class, copy/move and
constructor/assignment distinction plus unit locality. Unit-local families
get distinct compact `FamilyId`s; external families share ABI type identity
across units. Memoization is by typed class/form identity within a unit.
Streamed definitions retain only serialized spans and compact form/entry ranks;
typed function bodies are released as before. Final presentation performs the
C++ two passes over occupied family positions, retaining stable order within
a rank and every unrelated definition's position. It never reparses output,
retains function IR, or moves a backend lifetime into program scope.

The semantic ownership regression verifies unchanged expression/declaration
counts, preserved lvalue/category and independent conversion facts, constructor
memo reuse, an unprepared unused class and undemanded ambiguous-probe
constructors. Strict Clippy and the authoritative file audit pass with no new
lint expectations. No dependency, unsafe code, environment/process read,
textual phase transport, C++ fallback, test recognition, optimizer, allocator,
native generator, guard, oracle or protected-policy change is introduced.

Four supplemental required controls in PA12's executed lane pass course
outcomes and protected compiler byte comparison:

- `tests/controls/400-return-implicit-move-two-pass.t`: named parameters,
  parenthesized operands, nested locals, reference exclusion and ambiguous
  xvalue-probe fallback, with runtime copy/move counters.
- `tests/controls/400-return-selected-deleted-move-bad.t`: a selected deleted
  move rejects without retrying the available copy.
- `tests/controls/400-return-move-default-temporary-lifetime.t`: selected move
  defaults are alive during the transfer and gone before the caller continues.
- `tests/controls/400-special-member-family-demand-order.t`: synthesized base
  copy/move transfers and assignments, initially demanding move before copy.

## Explicit base initialization handoff (2026-10-15, unreviewed)

Group base: `8c43229b3f9213be9de147e18ab31b2f7fb6dc97`, clean on entry.
Stage base and last-audited commit above are unchanged. This is a coherent
partial implementation handoff; it does not complete PA12 or advance the stage.
The preceding named-return implementation is authoritative progress; the
turn-start required progress check nevertheless remained at 246/361. This
handoff reduces that baseline's fixed failures from **115 to 110**.

### C++ owners and translated responsibility

Consulted the frozen implementation directly:

- `sema/analyzer_construction.cpp::{initialize_class,direct_initialization,
  constructor_plan}`: retained mem-initializer syntax, class copy selection,
  base-object access context and destruction dependencies.
- `lower/object_lowering.cpp::{initialize_subobjects,construct_base,
  call_special,tail_occupied,subobject_bit_limit}`: base entries, empty/value
  initialization, storage transfers, occupied tail padding, prvalues and
  declaration-ordered completed-subobject cleanup.
- `lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp::{begin_initializer,note_call_arguments,
  begin_call,creates_temporaries}`: per-initializer expression regions,
  written/default argument demand and eager insertion positions.
- `lower/aggregate_lowering.cpp::copy_object`: stored class transfers own
  nvsize bytes; a value made to protect an occupied tail uses a separate slot.

Rust ownership is `sema/{construction,constructor_initialization,lowering}.rs`
and `lower/{base_initialization,construction,temporary_scan}.rs`, reusing the
existing aggregate, copy, materialization, reference and cleanup owners.
The source map records these responsibilities.

`ClassContext::Base` retains single same-class braced inputs as an original
list's `ClassInitialization::FromExpression(ExprId)`, while existing copy
selection records the source's `CopyAction` and selected `ConstructorId`.
Derived prvalue sources demand their actual temporary destruction facts.
Nothing reparses or grows syntax. Source type/value category remains intact.
The ownership regression verifies unchanged expression/declaration counts,
one selected copy identity shared across two base plans, a prvalue list
referring to its original operand, an external selected copy that stays
external and an unused class whose copy facts remain undemanded.

The constructor owner dispatches each base action to `initialize_base`.
Nontrivial copies/moves and trivial empty copies with lifetime effects call
C2. Other stored transfers bind the selected reference, preserving source
base projection, and copy at most nvsize. Own-class prvalues construct at the
base address. Where the enclosing base entry or a previously initialized base
occupies the tail, a nondestructible nonempty prvalue instead constructs in
`baseobj` and transfers only the base's owned span. Empty value initialization
writes no byte; other value initialization uses the recorded zero-before-call
fact and occupied-tail span. Aggregate base lists retain the existing member
initialization algorithm. Forwarded constructors retain their selected C2 call.

Each initializer records its cleanup floor before computing the base address,
ends its own temporaries, and only then registers the completed base for
subsequent constructor unwind. The temporary query distinguishes the base's
outer destination transfer from evaluating its source. Shared constructor
argument queries follow original written/default expressions only when a
pending hidden segment requires them. They preserve eager markers before base
addressing, avoid making the base itself an argument temporary, and close
transient call regions before default-argument destruction.

All transient addresses, query scratch and cleanup chains belong to one
`Function`; only existing typed LowIR/entry metadata escapes it. The base span
query walks preceding bases as C++ `tail_occupied` does, never unrelated
classes or functions. There is no new whole-program analysis, fallback,
textual transport, unsafe code, dependency, environment read or process access.
Optimizer, register allocator and native code owners are unchanged.

### Next dependency boundary and exact remaining identities

The nearby base-copy cases now expose a distinct **constructor bit-field
initialization order selected by assignment demand**. Both
`tests/general/300-base-tail-padding-copy.t` and
`tests/general/300-bit-field-copy-semantics.t` differ first in `Bits::Bits`:
C++ masks a whole unit before addressing it; a subsequent merge loads/clears
first, masks/shifts the new value, then recomputes the store address.
The transferred tail spans themselves match. Continue from
`lower/object_lowering.cpp::initialize_subobjects`'s `bit_order_alternate_`
and `lower/aggregate_lowering.cpp::initialize_bit_field` into the Rust
bit-field/member-initialization owners. Preserve the actual implicit assignment
demand fact independently of emission order, with an indexed class-owned fact
rather than a scan of all functions. Do not globally change PA11 field order.

Other remaining groups are allocation/new-array/delete, conversion selection
and diagnostics, class condition/reference/loop lifetimes, call destination
materialization, union identities and exception/definition-order details.
`remaining-groups.json` retains every fixed failure's raw diagnostic, earliest
observed category, trace, course log and mismatch path: 35 semantic/diagnostic
divergences, 25 raw artifact divergences and 50 explicit missing dependencies
(including 23 array-allocation/forwarding and 10 extended-expression delete
paths). These are trace clusters, not claims of a single root cause for every
artifact mismatch. The historical documented oracle exception-specification
finding remains separate; no oracle/reference/checker/fixture policy changed.

## Second independent checkpoint audit — 2026-10-15

Reviewed implementation commit: `ba8eecf466bafc7d17af6ec8ade10684f4c8a102` (including the audit corrections).
Audit entry/base: `e04cfec085389c122f579ca8ced50e5581c777e5`, clean, **251/361** fixed cases.
The review spans the complete diff since last audit `33834eb`, including the
historical `d295c54` audit record and accepted implementation checkpoints
`737e9e3`, `8c43229`, `e04cfec`. Stage base remains `b9f3dba`.
The preceding accepted turn is **progress**: its committed base-initialization
implementation and protected 246 -> 251 identity receipts establish the entry
state. This audit certifies those completed partial groups, not full PA12.
Ralph retains stage advancement and goal ownership; no Ralph state was edited.

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the handout, conversion plan,
ledger, source map and the changed Rust responsibilities. Consulted frozen
`3438f5a` C++ owners directly: `sema/analyzer_copy.cpp` (selection, parameters,
NothrowCopy and defaulted facts), `analyzer_bodies.cpp` (return eligibility and
two-pass move), `analyzer_construction.cpp` (initialization, access and plans),
`analyzer_expressions.cpp` (conversion identity), `lower/copy_lowering.cpp`,
`object_lowering.cpp`, `cleanup_lowering.cpp`, `tracked_lifetime_lowering.cpp`,
`function_lowering.cpp`, `statement_lowering.cpp` and `definition_order.cpp`.
Rust owners are the handoff owners above, with corrections in
`sema/assignment.rs` and `lower/{base_initialization,class_conditionals,
construction,lifetime,temporary_scan}.rs`. The source map is current.

### Findings, corrections and reproduced accepted-checkpoint regressions

1. **Assignment parameter-copy default arguments lost their exception fact.**
   A by-value subobject operator= whose selected copy/move constructor is
   noexcept but has omitted defaults incorrectly made its containing implicit
   assignment `unwind=no`. The frozen `NothrowCopy` assumes any omitted defaults
   can throw. The property now reads the selected canonical signature's arity,
   preserving explicitly written parent exception specifications. Scalar and
   class defaults, base/member/array parameters and copy/move selections match.
2. **Copy destinations were counted as source temporaries.** The named-return
   query added in `8c43229` also affected operand-only conditional queries;
   `e04cfec` queried a constructor's own selected destination transfer again as
   its argument. This forced whole-expression tracking for ordinary branch
   copies and extra eager return regions. Operand queries now exclude the
   root transfer's destination, constructor argument queries distinguish the
   same selected ConstructorId, and the return owner separately retains the
   recorded initialization conversion for its hidden segment. Defaults and
   actual source objects retain their lifetimes; result storage is never
   registered as a temporary owned by the callee.
3. **Comma base initializers delayed the outer region until after addressing.**
   An own-class prvalue such as `A((T(), A()))` was treated entirely as an
   in-place call. Its left operand already belongs to the mem-initializer's
   full-expression. The built-in comma now retains eager placement before
   the base address; overloaded comma remains governed by its selected call.
4. **Base conditional defaults crossed segment boundaries.** Untracked class
   conditional branch edges now close their segment before jumping. A
   constructor call outside a segment/construction closes a region it opens
   transiently before its temporary becomes live. Normal guarded destruction
   retains its enclosing-region continuation only when that region was active;
   an already closed branch region no longer adds a spurious empty continuation.
   The existing tracked and potentially throwing-destructor paths remain distinct.

These are byte/ownership findings, independent of fixed fixture counts.
A separate clean detached build of `e04cfec` reproduced failures in the earlier
`400-audit-conditional-copy-default-lifetime.t`,

### Algorithmic, ownership and source-responsibility audit

The completed assignment-parameter group has retained selected function/copy
edges and demanded constructor plans, including flattened small/large arrays,
caller-owned parameter destruction, constructor-default full-expressions and
exceptional cleanup. The return group checks original resolved binding/storage
and function ownership, keeps the original lvalue facts while ranking an xvalue
view, retries only a failed/ambiguous/differently typed probe, and checks access
and deletion on the retained selection. Discarded probes publish no callable
plan or emission. Resolution scratch is cleared and restored on errors.

Explicit base initialization consumes typed base/class layout, original
initializer/operand IDs, selected copy identity, value/zero facts and occupied
nonvirtual spans. It preserves source categories, empty evaluation, C2 entry
selection, safe tail copies, aggregate lists, prvalue destinations, per-initializer
temporary floors and declaration-ordered completed-base unwind. A failed
function drops its local region/cleanup state; branch counters are restored
before propagating lowering failures. No syntax or canonical type is cloned
or reparsed and no lookup/overload resolution is repeated in lowering.

Definition ordering uses stable class/form/entry identities, stable ranks at
occupied family positions and ABI identity only for cross-unit symbol families.
The two passes match C++ copy-before-move and base-before-complete ordering.
The 18 repeated two-unit receipts cover shared/external and anonymous/unit-local
families; unrelated definition positions and function-body streaming are retained.
All transient copy, array, temporary, handler and region data belongs to one
Function; serialized definition spans hold no frontend or function-IR owner.

Queries follow selected constructor/signature dependencies and class-owned
layout edges, not unrelated global functions. Canonical plans and class passing
facts are memoized; the existing semantic ownership tests verify parse-once
cardinality, reuse, discarded probes and unused owners. Base-initialization is
a cohesive owning algorithm, not a mechanical source split. Source limits and
responsibilities were checked by the authoritative companion audit, not inferred
from Clippy. No new lint expectations, blanket suppressions, dependencies,
unsafe paths, environment/process access, fixture recognition, textual phase
transport or C++ implementation fallback were introduced. Production tool names,
Rust naming, optimizer, register allocator and native owners are preserved.

Numeric storage spans, array thresholds/counters, target wrapping arithmetic,
bit-field transfer units, alignment, noalias/object extents and class ABI facts
remain those recorded by their typed owners. Replayed probes cover exact
stream/diagnostic bytes for private/deleted/explicit selections and exact LowIR
instruction/cleanup/emission order. Native backend equality is not claimed for
this PA12 frontend checkpoint; course execution uses the permitted frozen backend.

## Constructor bit-field initialization after implicit assignment demand

Group base: `6cd384b8cc4253a2363673813a183af5efa424b5`, clean checkout,
**252/361** fixed-manifest passes. The preceding reviewed checkpoint is
**progress**: its committed implementation and independent evidence establish
252 passes and identify this ordering boundary. Stage base and last-audited
commit at the top remain unchanged; implementation does not certify a stage
or advance the audit marker. PA12 is still partial.

### C++ owners, translated behavior and typed data flow

Consulted directly in the frozen read-only C++ checkout:

- `sema/analyzer_expressions.cpp::odr_use`: potentially evaluated function use
  records demand independently of emitted-body reachability.
- `sema/analyzer_copy.cpp::declare_implicit_copy_members`: only undeclared
  copy/move assignment members populate the class's implicit assignment slots.
- `lower/object_lowering.cpp::{initialize_subobjects,initialize_member}`:
  `bit_order_alternate_` reads those implicit members' actual demand marks,
  including marks from an uncalled inline member. Anonymous plans retain the
  enclosing constructor's order, resetting only allocation-unit progress.
- `lower/aggregate_lowering.cpp::initialize_bit_field`: alternate value-first
  initialization masks/shifts a fresh whole unit before computing its address;
  a merge computes its read address, loads/clears, masks/shifts/combines, then
  recomputes the complete store path. Packed/split and volatile unit operations
  and enclosing base-entry size limits remain shared with ordinary order.

Rust `sema/function_demands.rs::record_demand` publishes the monotonic
`assignment::ImplicitUse` on the owning canonical class in `sema/graph.rs`.
`assignment.rs::lower_implicit_assignment_demanded` exposes this fact by compact
`EntityId`. Recording does not follow emission queues, inspect unrelated
functions, or treat synthesis/property preparation as use. Unevaluated and
uninstantiated template expressions retain the existing demand guards;
user-declared and explicitly defaulted assignments do not set this fact.

`lower/construction.rs::initialize_constructor` reads the fact once into the
function-owned `bit_fields::InitializationOrder`. This order has the function's
lifetime and is independent of per-subobject unit/high/limit resets.
`member_initialization.rs` supplies a deferred address operation over original
member/class/type identities; it reproduces this-pointer reloads and anonymous
holder projections on each required address evaluation. `aggregates.rs` uses
the same operation for named value-first field initialization. This-rooted and
structured address-first aggregate stores retain their original order.
`bit_fields.rs::initialize_field_at` owns the whole/merge sequence, delegates
ordinary initialization to the existing routine, and shares conversion, mask,
split-unit load/store and storage-limit algorithms. No new optimization,
semantic lookup, AST copying, expression reparsing, textual transport or
backend behavior is introduced.

The semantic unit test verifies class-specific demand from an unused inline
member, repeated uses, exclusion of unevaluated and defaulted assignment,
absence of an implicit assignment root from that member, and unchanged syntax
node counts. It does not demand an unused class layout or body.
Two new PA12 executed controls cover runtime values, ordered side effects,
copy/move selection, base tail padding, signed/narrow/volatile/packed/split
fields, anonymous holders, zero-width separators, unused inline demand,
unevaluated/defaulted boundaries and nested aggregate order:

- `tests/controls/400-bit-field-constructor-assignment-demand.t`
- `tests/controls/400-bit-field-constructor-demand-boundaries.t`

Both pass protected raw comparison and their explicit exit-zero execution
outcomes. Existing fixtures and references were not edited or formatted.
No dependencies, lint expectations, unsafe paths, environment reads, compiler
fallbacks, test recognition, optimizer or register allocator changes were added.
The companion source audit verifies responsibilities/limits independently of
Clippy. Function-local order/address state is dropped with its body; only the
canonical demand fact lives with the semantic unit.

## Current union-place behavior group

This group starts at clean commit `b78a03b178321a32f4249c9ef029a16be8abb160`
and **254/361** fixed-manifest passes. The preceding goal turn is **progress**:
its committed assignment-demanded bit-field work and retained 252 → 254
comparison establish this group's next boundary. The stage base and last-audited
commit at the top remain unchanged. This is a coherent partial PA12 handoff;
Ralph retains stage advancement and independent checkpoint auditing.

### C++ owners and typed ownership review

Consulted directly in the frozen read-only checkout:

- `sema/analyzer_classes.cpp::anonymous_union`: injected bindings, one hidden
  storage object, namespace linkage and the hidden object's storage flags.
- `sema/constant_expressions.cpp::ConstantInterpreter::entity_place`: a named
  injected member designates its containing unnamed object recursively.
- `lower/expression_lowering.cpp::{variable_place,assignment}`: hidden storage
  selection, member byte projections, union memory facts, declaration publication
  errors and literal conversion context.
- `lower/program_lowering.cpp::{anonymous_object,simple_declaration}` and
  `lower/statement_lowering.cpp::{declaration_statement,local_object_variable}`:
  storage at the owning declaration and generated automatic slot identity.
- `lower/symbol_lowering.cpp::{FirstNamedMember,variable_symbol}`: preorder
  first-member ABI naming in the object's scope, without an internal marker.
- `lower/global_lowering.cpp::{static_global,define_global}` and
  `lower/local_static_lowering.cpp::{local_static,local_static_variable}`:
  constant images, constructor demand, dynamic startup/first-entry guards and
  anonymous static objects without fabricated source/ABI names.

Rust `sema/anonymous_types.rs` exposes the existing declaration-owned storage
binding by `DeclId`. An injected use retains both its original selected member
`BindingId` and hidden storage binding in `ExprFacts`; nested anonymous holders
follow their typed containment edges. `VariableFacts` carries `Option<NameId>`;
named-only clients use a checked accessor. No placeholder identifier is interned
as semantic identity. The first ABI member name comes from an iterative preorder
walk of only that anonymous class's declaration-ordered members. Named variables
retain their previous ABI identity publication order, including direct external
spellings. The frozen hidden-object path publishes no TLS flag; Rust preserves
that observable rule rather than applying the ordinary variable path's flag.

`sema/layout.rs::StorageDemand::AnonymousUnion` attaches layout and static-image
work to the actual enclosing function demand. Unused inline static storage stays
unlaid-out and has no image. `static_arrays.rs` records supported selected
scalar/array default-member images using the existing target conversion and
symbolic-address routines. A nonconstant initializer keeps ordinary dynamic
construction. General interpreted constexpr class construction remains a later
semantic boundary. `constant_addresses.rs` demands the hidden root's layout and
uses the same complete holder offset as runtime projection, including a global
pointer/reference to an injected nested union member eight bytes into its root.

`lower/function.rs` initializes a hidden automatic object through the same
entity-owned local construction/lifetime path as a named object. It allocates
one generated union slot without synthesizing an `InitDeclarator` or reparsing
syntax. `mutations.rs` projects each injected use from that slot or its global,
retains untyped union memory, and handles arrays/references through the existing
place operations. A block static already published by an enclosing emitted
function is reached through its canonical symbol. Missing automatic/static
publication preserves the frozen diagnostic. A nonbraced direct union-member
assignment uses initialization conversion context; braced assignment unwraps its
original clause in assignment context. Compound, indirect and nested-struct
member assignments keep their own contexts.

`globals.rs` shares definition, startup and finalizer handling between named and
hidden declarations. A constant default initialization emits its selected helper
without an extra empty startup function. `local_storage.rs` uses one static
object and, for a nonconstant default initializer, one first-entry guard, with
all construction state owned by the current function. `symbols.rs` memoizes its
canonical declaration and preserves source ordinal, internal/weak binding and
absent ABI name. The unit's existing pending-emission loop is now a separate
cohesive function in `lower/mod.rs`; the same emission sets move into it and each
body is streamed/released in the same order. No optimizer, allocator, native
emitter, stack guard, dependency or production process/environment access changes.

The semantic regression checks unchanged syntax counts, shared hidden/member
identity, preorder first-name selection, a symbolic pointer at the full nested
offset, direct union assignment classification and an unused inline static
object with neither layout nor constant image. Strict quality and the companion
file audit cover the modified owners; no lint expectation or suppression was added.

### Preserved frozen limitations and reproducer

The valid source `int f(){union{unsigned x:3;long y;};x=7;return x;}` is rejected
by the frozen compiler with exit 1 and `a member without a byte offset` at its
first use of `x`. `probes/bitfield-block.cpp`, the corresponding protected
receipt in `probe-results.json` and `oracle-bitfield-host.json` retain the
reproducer, matching Rust/C++ diagnostics and a successful host
`g++ -std=c++11 -fsyntax-only` check. C++ `bit_field_access` recognizes an
id-expression only with implicit-this facts, so this injected name follows
ordinary `variable_place` and its byte-offset requirement. Rust preserves that
ordinary rejection; it does not substitute status 86 or claim this accepted
bit-field surface is implemented. This is reported as a frozen oracle limitation,
without changing the source, references, fixtures or acceptance policy.

Likewise, jumping over an anonymous union declaration and then using its injected
member is rejected by the frozen lowering: the unreachable declaration lane
visits written declarators but omits the hidden object. Automatic storage reports
`a variable outside the procedural lowering slice`; static storage reports
`a static local variable of a function not lowered first`. The two standalone
sources and protected receipts are in `unreachable.py`, `probes/unreachable*.cpp`
and `unreachable.jsonl`. The final Rust path preserves these exact publication
errors. Independent union storage, conversion and constant-address work continued.
The earlier explicitly-noexcept defaulted-copy oracle defect remains documented
above, with no waiver or reference change.

### Union resource measurements and protected stack qualification

`measure.py` generates 100/400/1,600 supported namespace/function owners, each
with one global anonymous union and one automatic anonymous union. The function
assigns a widening literal to the global member, copies its scalar value into
the local member and reads it for its result. All three complete LowIR outputs
match through the protected adapter (`scaling.jsonl`). Measurements run after
the validation processes finish: oracle A/A calibration, then two paired
candidate/oracle/oracle/candidate blocks, four measured observations per tool
and size. Times include process startup with warm filesystem caches; RSS is the
largest observed peak. `resources.json`, `scaling/` and `measure.log` retain
sources, raw timings, streams, sizes and output hashes; all 30 runs exit zero
and agree with the corresponding protected output hash.

| Namespace/function owners | Rust mean ms / peak RSS KiB | C++ mean ms / peak RSS KiB |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| 100 | 31.24 / 8836 | 11.19 / 8704 |
| 400 | 64.95 / 14360 | 23.19 / 12756 |
| 1600 | 195.63 / 41032 | 77.93 / 27596 |

For 16x as many owners, observed Rust time grows 6.26x and peak RSS 4.64x.
These are supported observations, not a universal complexity proof or a
PA35 generated-code improvement claim. Each use follows only its selected
anonymous-holder edges; first-name traversal visits only that object's class,
and transient local/guard state remains per function.

`scaling/nested-union.cpp` uses one fixed 512-parenthesis assignment operand.
At a common initial **1 MiB** stack, the Rust byte-budget guard rejects it.
The protected `stack.jsonl` receipt reports **match=true** and
**stack_qualification.passed=true**: both tools succeed with identical streams,
deterministic counters and output hashes at common **16 MiB**. Raw initial and
qualified attempts remain in
`stack-qualifications/04735e0db6e0478bb2ed0af0d48aa80b/`.
Acceptance reads the qualification receipt, not the preserved initial candidate
exit or exhaustion diagnostic position. No guard or physical cutoff changed.

assignment conversion context and shared constant-address ownership for the
supported group, including namespace/block/static declaration boundaries.

need typed selected deallocation/destructor facts and explicit-storage cleanup

Array allocation/counts/cookies/deletion remain a distinct related extension.
Other residual groups are conversion functions and ranking, class parameter
slicing, reference/condition/loop lifetimes, result destination materialization,
class/list argument boundaries and complete-class exception queries. General
interpreted constexpr class/default initialization remains separate from this
group's selected scalar/array static images. No stage-completion claim follows
from the three gained fixtures.

## PA12 scalar explicit-storage group (implementation handoff)

Group base: `9aeb6defbaf6f07101029dadcae33c5e5ae87da8`, clean entry,
**257/361** fixed-manifest passes. The preceding anonymous-union goal turn was
**progress**, established by that implementation commit and its recorded
254 → 257 identity comparison. This group leaves the stage base and
last-audited commit above unchanged. It is a coherent partial implementation
handoff; Ralph owns checkpoint review and stage advancement.

### Consulted C++ owners and earliest divergence

Read directly in frozen C++ `3438f5aea24d8fcc9a0113316f7e27c890cd5ac6`:

- `sema/analyzer_construction.cpp::{deletion,usual_deallocation,
  new_deallocation,check_destruction,allocation}`: resolved object-pointer
  operands, incomplete-class handling, usual versus placement deallocation,
  access/deletion checks and semantic demand.
- `sema/analyzer_exceptions.cpp::may_throw`: deletion's selected deallocator,
  pointee destructor and operand; the existing allocation query's intentional
  distinction from lowering's constructor-default query.
- `lower/allocation_lowering.cpp::{deletion,destroy_deleted,deleted_storage,
  free_storage,allocation,allocation_initializer,initialization_may_throw}`:
  explicit storage, exact null branches, sized arguments, destructor calls,
  initializer-failure cleanup and saved placement values.
- `lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp::{may_throw,creates_temporaries,split_region,
  region_event,emit_call,chain_entry}` and
  `lower/object_lowering.cpp::{temporary,destroy_local}`: cleanup chain
  identities, temporary addressing, region placement, spill order and storage
  deallocation during unwinding. `lower/aggregate_lowering.cpp::end_counted`
  and omitted member initialization establish cleanup removal and deferred
  handler placement.

The seven gained cases all first failed in Rust semantic expression dispatch
with status **86**, `extended semantic expressions`: the allocation control
also contains `delete`. The existing scalar-allocation failure path independently
returned status 86 when initialization could throw. Raw turn-start traces and
streams, rather than the presence of an old output file, establish these
boundaries. No status-86 outcome was accepted as a rejecting compiler.

### Rust ownership and completed behavior

`sema/allocation.rs` owns selection, usual/placement matching and allocation
throw queries. `sema/{expression_facts,lowering}.rs` own sparse original-ExprId
`Deletion` facts and their read-only view: selected `BindingId`/`EntityId`,
canonical cv-qualified `TypeId` and incomplete-object status. Parentheses share
facts; the pointer operand retains its source category and a noninitializing
standard conversion. The semantic query consumes these facts, without another
lookup, syntax construction or overload search.

`lower/allocation.rs` consumes the selected use. Complete-class scalar deletion
checks null, invokes a nontrivial complete destructor even if its body is empty,
then deallocates the storage. Scalar and incomplete-class pointers follow the
frozen direct-deallocation form. A sized member receives the object size;
explicit global qualification bypasses class deallocation lookup. Private,
deleted and invalid-pointer uses retain the oracle's regular diagnostics.

A throwing deleted destructor temporarily adds one
`CleanupPlace::Deallocation` to the existing completed-object chain. This
address-owned action frees storage during unwinding, without incorrectly
running the object's destructor again or adding a destructor-termination guard
around deallocation. On success it leaves the chain before ordinary free.

Scalar new uses that same cleanup action while initialization can throw.
The original evaluated placement Values are retained once as a typed,
function-owned LowIR argument slice and reused by placement deallocation.
Usual new/delete matching shares the one-parameter-before-class-size_t rule;
placement matching still checks the allocation signature and variadicness.
Reserved placement new and nonthrowing null allocation keep their existing
rules. Aggregate initialization prepares only the reached class's destructor
identities; it does not impose complete-object destructor access on scalar new.
Completed aggregate members, constructor defaults and initializer temporaries
retain their ordinary ownership.

`lower/{lifetime,lifetime_regions,materialization,temporary_scan,throw_scan}.rs`
keep all transient state per function. A storage split opens after a temporary's
address; its completion region targets the prefix beneath surviving initializer
temporaries. Omitted aggregate class-member initialization defers its already
allocated handler records until final function placement, preserving stores and
block order. Delete temporary scans visit the original operand; incomplete
classes with no destructor do not require completed destructor properties.

The semantic ownership regression verifies unchanged syntax/declaration counts,
original parenthesized pointers, noninitializing conversions, class-sized versus
global deallocator identity, incomplete-class state and an untouched unused
class. All added selection work is bounded by the looked-up overload set or the
reached containment graph. No unit-wide scan, text transport, new dependency,
unsafe code, environment/process read, compiler fallback, fixture recognition,
optimizer or backend change is introduced. The authoritative file audit remains
required alongside strict Clippy; no lint expectation was added.

### Resource observations and stack qualification

`measure.py` generates supported fixed inputs with 100/400/1600 separately
emitted functions, each deleting a possibly throwing-destructor object,
allocating with a constructor-argument temporary and deleting the result.
`scaling.jsonl` proves successful raw byte equality for all three inputs through
the protected adapter. After validation jobs finish, A/A oracle calibration and
two ABBA blocks collect four observations per tool/input. `resources.json`
retains source sizes, all times/RSS, exits, streams and output hashes;
`resources-final.log` retains the summary. These are compile measurements,
including process startup, not PA35 generated-code improvement requirements.

| Bodies | Rust mean ms / peak RSS KiB | C++ mean ms / peak RSS KiB |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| 100 | 27.07 / 8036 | 9.62 / 8672 |
| 400 | 53.79 / 9628 | 19.52 / 9972 |
| 1600 | 165.44 / 22864 | 64.42 / 16288 |

For 16x as many bodies, observed Rust time grows **6.11x**,
peak RSS **2.85x**. These observations support this workload;
they are not a universal complexity guarantee.


The fixed 512-parenthesis delete operand uses an initial common **1 MiB**
stack. Its `stack.jsonl` receipt has **match=true** and
**stack_qualification.passed=true**: both tools succeed with identical streams
and output hash at common **16 MiB**. Raw initial and qualified attempts are
retained beneath `stack-qualifications/`; `stack-final.log` records the receipt.
The adapter deliberately preserves initial candidate exit/streams/files, so
acceptance reads qualification `match`. No native cutoff, diagnostic position,
stack guard or safety byte budget was calibrated or changed.

cleanup group. Array allocation/deletion is the next related owner boundary:

and `lower/allocation_lowering.cpp::{array_allocation,array_cookie,deletion}`
need a typed runtime count/shape, class array allocation/deallocation keys,
length checks, cookies, flattened element loops and partial-array cleanup.
Class-to-pointer delete operands separately require the existing conversion
function candidate/application machinery, which remains incomplete. Virtual
deletion remains PA13 work.

The constant evaluator still rejects `static_assert(noexcept(0),"p")`
without typing its query, independently of delete; the same boundary affects
`static_assert(!noexcept(delete (A*)0),"p")`. These are candidate gaps, not
oracle defects or waived matches. `boundaries-final.log`, `boundaries.jsonl`
and `probes/{constant-noexcept,delete-constant-noexcept}.cpp` retain both
standalone reproducers and exact diagnostics. Ordinary typed delete-noexcept
return expressions are covered by the matching group probes; constant-query
entry preparation remains an independent next semantic boundary.

**264/361** fixed-manifest passes. Previous last-audited implementation:
`ba8eecf466bafc7d17af6ec8ade10684f4c8a102`. Reviewed its complete diff through
entry, including accepted checkpoints `b78a03b`, `9aeb6de` and `529d625`.
The preceding turn is **progress**, established by those committed groups and
retained identity evidence. Reviewed implementation including audit fixes:
`3335e750c36a9a56689943965b3e3b9bf13f82d5`; the last-audited marker advances to

checkpoint, not full PA12 completion. Ralph owns phase/stage advancement.

### Scope, frozen owners and findings

Read `AGENTS.md`, `CLONE_CONTRACT.md`, `spec.md`, `docs/rust-clone-plan.md`,
`pa12/README.md`, this ledger and the Rust source map. Consulted frozen C++
commit `3438f5aea24d8fcc9a0113316f7e27c890cd5ac6` directly, particularly:

- `sema/analyzer_expressions.cpp::odr_use`, `sema/analyzer_copy.cpp` and
  `lower/{object,aggregate}_lowering.cpp`: implicit-assignment demand versus
  emission, bit-field whole/merge order, conversion/masking, storage limits
  and anonymous subobject address evaluation.
- `sema/analyzer_classes.cpp::anonymous_union`,
  `sema/constant_expressions.cpp::entity_place`, and
  `lower/{expression,program,symbol,global,local_static,statement}_lowering.cpp`:
  injected bindings, hidden storage, naming, constant images, publication,
  initialization and linkage.
- `sema/analyzer_construction.cpp::{allocation,deletion,usual_deallocation,
  new_deallocation,check_destruction}`, `sema/analyzer_exceptions.cpp::may_throw`,
  `lower/allocation_lowering.cpp` and
  `lower/{cleanup,tracked_lifetime,condition,object,aggregate}_lowering.cpp`:
  selected deallocator identity, null tests, placement values, completed-object
  cleanup, initializer temporaries, hidden segments and deferred handlers.

Fresh baseline reproduction built accepted `529d625` in an isolated detached
worktree, with its own Cargo target. Its binary SHA-256 exactly reproduces the
recorded entry binary
`73f5f970ae4b1116025641de8d0449a9c3b25f5e86ebdd0aedba468464f43549`.
`baseline-build.log`, `baseline-probes.json` and `baseline-probes.log` reproduce
**ten** shallow standalone divergences in the accepted groups; final probes
match all ten. The audit worktree was removed after reproduction. Findings:

1. Hidden union storage incorrectly inherited a surrounding `extern "C"`.
   C++ creates this hidden object with ordinary C++ language identity. Rust now
   preserves its object name and metadata without changing injected bindings.
2. An explicitly throwing scalar deallocation function could open a transient
   region without closing it or placing its pending local-destruction chain.
   `deallocate_storage` now closes that transient call's region. The ordinary
   throw scan retains C++'s deliberate exclusion of the deallocator itself;
   semantic noexcept queries and the emitted call still use its specification.
3. Nonthrowing scalar new failed to track initializer temporaries made only
   when allocation returned nonnull. This could destroy an object never made,
   and the full-expression's final chain could lose the storage cleanup needed
   while construction was in progress. Original initializer facts now trigger
   guarded tracking for the null-tested, nonreserved form. A typed
   `ConstructionTracking` state separates storage-construction chains from the
   final conditional chain; construction failure frees storage, successful
   completion removes that action, and each surviving temporary is destroyed
   under the prefix beneath it. Actual noexcept facts remain distinct from
   hidden full-expression placement and call-result spills.
4. The allocated object's own braced initializer must not itself be counted as
   an initializer temporary. The guard query excludes that root's destination
   while still visiting its operands. Plain and hidden throw queries consume
   selected call facts explicitly; nonthrowing allocation under a hidden return
   segment retains the frozen spill without fabricating an extra cleanup flag.
5. Scalar conditional values lacked their guard-reset insertion boundary and
   second-operand region reopening. Delete operands containing conditional
   temporaries returned status 86; conditional allocations also had different
   region order. The function-owned guard boundary and monotonic region ordinal
   now preserve both operands' ordered effects. A delete expression starts its
   operand normally; a trivial containing class prvalue still opens a pending
   region for its argument temporaries. Nested `delete new` now retains the
   correct address, construction, null-test and cleanup order.
6. An allocated aggregate binding a reference member to a class prvalue could
   reach lowering without that temporary's layout (internal-error exit 70).
   The original checked reference-binding/destruction use now queues its class
   layout on the reached function. A semantic ownership regression proves
   unchanged syntax counts, both required ready layouts and an untouched unused
   class. This also fixes the previously failing fixed case
   `tests/general/500-direct-class-call-temporary-destination.t`.
7. Omitted aggregate members whose constructor defaults make temporaries used
   the wrong address/region and handler order. Per-function omitted-handler
   storage now resumes ahead of subsequently created handlers when those
   temporaries require it; otherwise deferred placement stays at function end.
   Explicit element-temporary scopes save, clear and restore their storage-split
   boundary, preventing an inner construction from inheriting an outer split.

Eight added executed PA12 controls cover these findings: `400-anonymous-union-language-linkage.t`, `400-scalar-delete-throwing-deallocator-region.t`,
`400-scalar-delete-member-prvalue-temporaries.t`,
`400-scalar-new-null-initializer-temporaries.t`,
`400-scalar-new-reference-member-temporary.t`,
`400-scalar-new-omitted-member-default-temporary.t`,
`400-scalar-new-own-list-is-not-temporary.t` and
`400-scalar-storage-conditional-operands.t`, all under `tests/controls/`.
Every control requires exit zero and also passes protected raw byte comparison.
No frozen fixture, reference, manifest, gate policy or Ralph state was edited.

### Algorithm, ownership and architecture review

The bit-field group correctly reads one class-owned implicit-assignment use
fact, independent of emitted-body reachability; repeated/unevaluated/defaulted
uses preserve their distinct demand rules. Deferred address operations preserve
whole-unit versus merge instruction order. Numeric conversion, masks, signed
fields, volatile/split units and base tail-padding limits continue to use the
shared field algorithms. There is no fixture-dependent alternate algorithm.

An anonymous union retains one declaration-owned hidden object. Original member
`BindingId` and storage binding facts preserve nested offsets and original cv/
category; typed member/class containment edges determine addresses. Namespace
ABI naming visits only its own declaration-ordered members. Block static symbols
are memoized by entity; reached function edges gate layout/images. Missing
publication and bit-field byte-offset errors retain frozen regular diagnostics.
Image building uses existing target conversion and symbolic-address routines;
constructor demand remains separate from constant storage initialization.

Scalar new/delete retain original operands, canonical `TypeId`, selected
`BindingId`/`EntityId` and complete/incomplete status. Selection/access/deletion
errors occur in semantics; lowering never reruns lookup. Usual deallocation
prefers one parameter before class-sized form; placement matching retains exact
parameter identity/variadicness and evaluated argument Values once. Cleanup
records own storage actions without re-destroying the object. Completed members,
reference temporaries and null-path flags retain separate ownership and order.
Property/layout demand stays on canonical entities and reached function edges;
unused classes, implicit helpers and bodies remain undemanded.

All new guard/reset/region/handler/argument state belongs to one function and is
released with its LowIR body. Explicit temporary scopes restore parent state.
The existing unit emission identities deduplicate streamed bodies; `emit_pending`
remains the same demand worklist, with its cohesive extraction reviewed against
its prior implementation. No semantic trees are copied or reparsed, no LowIR
text transports phase state, and no per-function work scans unrelated global
entities. No new dependency, unsafe path, fallback compiler, test recognition,
environment/process read, lint suppression, mechanical file split, optimizer,
register allocator or native code-generator change is introduced. Production tool
names stay compatible and new Rust identifiers follow Rust naming conventions.
The source map records the changed responsibilities; file audit and Clippy
independently pass. Templates/native ELF generation remain later-stage owners;
this audit does not claim to implement them through a frontend checkpoint.

and two paired ABBA blocks retain four observations per tool/input, every
exit/stream/output hash and peak RSS in `resources.json` and `scaling/`.

For 16x as many bodies, Rust observed time grows **5.90x** and peak RSS
**2.83x**. These startup-inclusive observations support this supported workload,
not a universal complexity proof or a PA35 optimization claim.

`stack.py` uses the fixed 512-parenthesis scalar-delete operand and a common
initial **1 MiB** native stack. The protected adapter receipt in `stack.jsonl`
has **match=true**, **stack_qualification.passed=true**: both tools succeed at
a common **16 MiB** with identical streams, deterministic counters and generated
output. Raw initial/qualified attempts remain under
`stack-qualifications/248d621d71874709a9af3be3a044bf8f/`; `stack-final.log`
retains the receipt. Acceptance reads the qualified `match`, since the adapter
preserves the original guarded candidate result. No exhaustion cutoff,
diagnostic position, stack guard or byte-budget limit was calibrated or changed.

### Remaining dependency boundary and exact failures

Array new/delete (runtime shape/count, overflow/length checks, cookies, element
and partial-prefix cleanup) remains the next explicit-storage boundary.
Class-to-pointer delete operands require the unfinished conversion-function
machinery. Other residual groups remain class/list/conversion ranking, slicing,
condition/loop/reference lifetimes and complete-class exception queries.

A separate shallow probe `probes/new-nothrow-own-copy.cpp` uses
`new A{A()}` with a user destructor and class allocation functions. Constructor
list selection still rejects it with `no viable function for the call` before
lowering, while C++ accepts it. `boundary-baseline.json` reproduces the identical
exit-1 diagnostic using the freshly rebuilt accepted implementation; initial
protected evidence is retained in `probes-own-list.log` / `probes.jsonl`.
This is the unfinished list constructor-selection boundary, not a completed
allocation-cleanup parity claim or an accepted rejection. The scalar cleanup
fixes do not replace its missing semantic selection. Earlier documented constant
noexcept entry preparation and explicitly-noexcept defaulted-copy oracle defects
also remain as recorded; no baseline or policy was changed.
