## Scalar-array explicit-storage checkpoint

This implementation group starts from clean `40ff8ccbd9013a06cff9d8a53d3348d92158c4a1`
and **265/361** passes. The preceding goal turn is **progress**: its committed
independent audit established the accepted scalar-storage scope and its next
array-storage boundary. Stage base and last-audited commit above are unchanged;
this implementation does not certify advancement or the complete assignment.

### Failure cluster and translated owners

The array-storage failures diverged first in semantic allocation/deletion:
array forms returned status 86 before allocation selection or lowering. The
shared frozen owners were consulted directly:

- `sema/analyzer_construction.cpp::{allocation,new_deallocation,deletion,
  usual_deallocation}` and `sema/analyzer_initialization.cpp::array_new_list`:
  first-bound typing/promotion, validation order, allocation lookup, initialization
  prefix and deallocation selection.
- `lower/allocation_lowering.cpp::{array_new_shape,count_may_be_invalid,
  length_may_throw,invalid_length,array_allocation,new_list_elements,zero_bytes,
  deletion}`: request arithmetic, length checks, null handling, prefix/tail work,
  matching storage cleanup and deallocation.
- `lower/aggregate_lowering.cpp::{aggregate_elements,aggregate_item,path_address}`
  and `lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp::{may_throw,emit_call,open_region}`: typed
  element projections, literal conversions and nested region ownership.
- `lower/program_lowering.cpp::resolve_singleton_roles`,
  `lower/function_lowering.cpp::lower_forwarder` and
  `lower/rtti_lowering.cpp::runtime_function`: runtime role identity, allocator
  replacement/forwarding and the demanded bad-array-length entry.

Rust owners are `sema/{allocation,array_allocation,declarators,lowering}.rs`,
`lower/{array_allocation,allocation,allocation_runtime,aggregates,symbols,
exception_helpers,lifetime,lifetime_regions,throw_scan,temporary_scan,mod}.rs`.
The source map records the responsibilities and explicit remaining boundaries.

The completed group covers arrays of scalar and pointer elements, including
nested array typedefs and character-string prefixes. First-bound diagnostics
precede target lookup; negative and over-limit constants reject normally,
non-integral/scoped-enum bounds reject normally, and list overflow/narrowing
retains the frozen spelling and location. An omitted initializer preserves
storage; empty parentheses/braces zero the tail; explicit clauses initialize
only their deduced prefix. A constant expression validates bounds, but its size
is emitted as arithmetic unless the original occurrence is literal or already
folded. This preserves the frozen O0 producer rather than adding constant folding.

Runtime counts widen by promoted signedness to target `std::size_t` before
multiplication. A single unsigned comparison covers both the prefix lower bound
and `PTRDIFF_MAX / stride` upper bound when the promoted type requires a check.
The invalid branch demands `bad_array_new_length`, or returns null for a custom
nonthrowing allocator. Placement operands are evaluated once and retained as
Values for matching cleanup; null allocation skips initialization. Prefix stores
use typed array projections, including index zero and nested object-array steps;
tail zeroing keeps the byte loop and exact memory flags. Scalar/pointer
`delete[]` calls the selected array deallocator with the original pointer.

The runtime gives each allocation/free role one demanded owner. A scalar
replacement wins over an undefined array form; a secondary array declaration
becomes an internal direct forwarding body, with positional parameters and the
original unwind boundary. Definition and declaration ordering matches single
and multiple source units, including replacements defined in a later unit.

### Ownership and architecture review

`ArrayAllocation` stores the original `ExprId`, an optional immediate count and
a canonical fixed-prefix `TypeId`. The allocated target remains the first
bound's element type; no runtime-sized semantic type, synthetic syntax, duplicate
semantic tree or rendered-text transport is introduced. Existing aggregate
analysis checks original list nodes once. The ownership unit test verifies
unchanged expression-store size, original lvalue bound facts, the nested prefix's
canonical element identity and an unused class whose layout remains unprepared.

An explicit array-storage path root reuses the aggregate path algorithm while
retaining the frozen typed index steps and initialization conversion context.
It acquires no automatic-object lifetime. Bound/length throw facts and bound
operand temporaries feed the existing query worklists. `CallRegion` distinguishes
a newly opened transient call region from an enclosing storage-initializer
region; nested allocations and calls leave the latter open until its owner
finishes. Spill rules remain separate from that region ownership.

Only demanded allocation-symbol records participate in singleton-role resolution;
there is no speculative scan of unrelated semantic entities. Runtime identities
belong to the program, while paths, placement values, cleanup chains and each
forwarding builder belong to one function and are released after streaming.
No dependency, unsafe/FFI path, environment/process access, fixture recognition,
fallback compiler, lint suppression, mechanical file split, optimizer, register
allocator or native emission change is introduced. Production symbol spellings
are ABI contracts; new Rust implementation names follow Rust conventions.

## Class-array explicit-storage implementation checkpoint

Group base: `91a50206fe0e12d51ce5f19b55a27f57a018bf36` (clean on entry).
The stage base and last audited commit at the top remain unchanged. This is an
implementation handoff; Ralph owns the independent audit and advancement.
The preceding required stage-progress attempt was **no progress**: its log
reported the same 274/361 and 87 failures. Re-reading the current traces showed
15 cases sharing the class-array construction stub. The apparent condition
lifetime cluster first depends on unfinished conversion selection, so the
independent explicit-storage group was translated next.

### Frozen owners and completed behavior

Consulted directly in `/home/vishvananda/work/references/v5rust-cpp/dev/src/`:

- `sema/analyzer_construction.cpp::{allocation,new_deallocation,
  sized_array_delete,deletion,check_destruction}` and
  `sema/analyzer_initialization.cpp::{array_new_list,implicit_constructor}`:
  element-class lookup, trailing/default selection, destructor availability and
  access, and class usual sized deallocation independently of `::` lookup.
- `sema/analyzer_access.cpp::check_special_access`: the class of the object
  whose lifetime ends participates in protected destructor access.
- `lower/allocation_lowering.cpp::{array_new_shape,array_cookie,
  array_allocation,new_list_elements,construct_new_elements,deletion}`:
  flattened counts, cookies, size limits, list prefixes, construction and
  reverse destruction, allocation-pointer recovery and unwind deallocation.
- `lower/aggregate_lowering.cpp::{path_address,element_step,aggregate_elements,
  aggregate_class_item,push_counted,element_made,begin_row,end_row}` and
  `lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp::{creates_temporaries,element_temporaries,
  begin_element_temporaries,end_element_temporaries}`: typed prefix paths,
  completed-prefix counters, row reuse and element default-argument lifetimes.

Rust semantic owners are `sema/{allocation,array_allocation,construction,
expression_facts,lowering}.rs`. Sparse allocation/deletion records retain the
selected `BindingId`, original target `TypeId`, optional `ConstructorId`,
original bound `ExprId`, canonical fixed-prefix array type and a separate
`sized_array_delete` fact. Lowering reads direct `EntityId` views. A filled
constant prefix does not select or demand a deleted default constructor. A
zero-length array preserves the oracle's available empty default run; an empty
list with a deleted default instead has no such run. All class-array uses
check destruction at the storage site, including private/friend/member and
protected-object contexts. Nothing is reparsed or synthesized as syntax.

Rust lowering owners are `lower/{array_allocation,array_initialization,
array_construction,array_deletion,aggregates,aggregate_arrays,aggregate_cleanup,
construction,element_temporaries,lifetime_regions,temporary_scan}.rs`.
The three new modules own cookie/prefix initialization, constructor attempts,
and deletion/destruction respectively; they are responsibility boundaries,
not mechanical source fragments. `source-map.tsv` records these owners.

The innermost element and product of inner dimensions determine request stride
and cookie counts. Cookies use max(8, element alignment), retain their flattened
count at the last size_t, and disappear only for the reserved namespace placement
signature or when destruction and usual sized deletion require none. Static
class placement functions keep their cookies. The runtime limit includes the
cookie even when a constant element-byte count passed semantic validation.
Nothrow allocation retains both invalid-length null and returned-null paths.
Constant element counts use the frozen `const i64` form. Target products,
subtractions and negative offsets use explicit wrapping arithmetic.

Listed prefixes consume the original checked list through the shared typed path
algorithm, preserving index zero, row/object projections, size-one scaling,
member evaluation order and temporary lifetimes. Destructible prefixes retain
one completed-element counter, reusable row counters when temporary creation
requires them, and transfer ownership from completed subobjects to the array.
An aggregate element already covered by a count does not register another
complete-element address. A constructor prefix owns its region across calls;
ordinary scalar initializer calls retain their transient call regions.

Trailing elements are zeroed only under the recorded value-initialization
rules, then constructed in a runtime loop, including the empty loop for a
zero-length default run. Each attempt ends its default-argument temporaries.
A failed attempt destroys the completed prefix in reverse order and invokes
the selected usual/placement deallocator with the original allocation size or
placement values. A throwing unwind destructor uses the existing terminate
handling. Default constructors with caller-owned by-value arguments contribute
their temporary facts before the allocation result's spill decision.

Delete[] recovers the original allocation pointer and count from the cookie,
computes a sized deallocation request, decrements its counter before each
complete-element destructor and frees storage after the elements left during
unwind. Normal delete and constructor-failure destruction share one loop
algorithm with an explicit `Destruction` mode, preserving their distinct call
regions and block spellings. Null, trivial, incomplete, globally qualified,
multidimensional and sized-delete paths keep the oracle's emission order.

All counters, storage values, default-argument scopes, regions, path steps and
cleanup records belong to the current function. Published semantic facts and
layouts remain unit-owned; only demanded classes/constructors/destructors are
prepared. The new semantic invariant test proves no AST/declaration growth,
no default demand for a filled prefix with a deleted default constructor,
retained prefix/cookie identities and no layout demand for an unrelated class.
There are no new dependencies, unsafe/FFI/process/environment paths, string
semantic keys, optimizer changes, register-allocation changes or native writer
changes. Frozen inputs, fixtures, references, checker policy and Ralph state
were not edited.

## Full-expression parameter ownership checkpoint

Group base: `d43110c0d3dfb298141857c60f72996422c55b51`. Stage base and
last-audited commit at the top remain unchanged. This is a coherent partial
implementation handoff; PA12 is not complete. The preceding class-array turn
was **progress**, demonstrated by its committed 274 → 289 result. This group
starts from the independently recorded 289/361 baseline, not from its new probes.

### Failure cluster, C++ owners and typed flow

The three cases below first diverged in unoptimized lowering: both tools
accepted them, and protected traces exposed different temporary destruction
edges, missing constructor-argument segments and displaced call regions.
The shared owner is the full-expression cleanup machinery, rather than array
storage or conversion-function selection. The frozen sources were consulted
directly at `/home/vishvananda/work/references/v5rust-cpp`:

- `lower/condition_lowering.cpp::{logical_value,logical_step}` records a
  right branch's cleanup floor after evaluating its left operand.
- `lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp::{invokes_caller_destroyed,caller_destroys,
  creates_temporaries,begin_full_expression,temporary_region,region_event,
  paused_call_region,before_throwing_call,close_region,chain_entry,
  drop_segment_floor,destroy_temporaries}` owns parameter discovery, region
  placement, segment floors and their invalidation.
- `lower/expression_lowering.cpp::argument`,
  `lower/object_lowering.cpp::{call_special,local_object}` and
  `lower/function_lowering.cpp::terminate` establish the storage/call/branch
  ordering consumed by that cleanup owner.

Rust owners are `lower/{conditions,temporary_scan,lifetime,lifetime_regions,
materialization,function}.rs` and `lowir/builder.rs`; the source map records
these responsibilities. Temporary queries consume original `ExprId`, selected
`ConstructorId`, canonical `TypeId`, declared parameter types and recorded
nontrivial-destructor facts. The shared signature query detects caller-owned
parameters on constructor/list/converting calls even when the destination
has no destructor. Operand-only queries exclude the destination while retaining
its parameters. References contribute no parameter object; a by-value class
with a nontrivial destructor has the existing indirect ABI boundary.

A logical operator retains every temporary created by its left operand across
both branch edges. Only the right operand's own temporaries can end on its
local branch. Caller-owned parameter objects retain their existing storage,
completion point and reverse full-expression destruction order.

`TemporarySegment::Nothrow { floor }` keeps the segment's initial cleanup
depth. Its ordinary unwind suffix stops there, excluding outer automatic
objects. Before a call or temporary destructor can throw, and when the segment
ends, the floor is dropped and affected cached unwind suffixes are cleared.
Throwing destruction therefore sees every object under the temporary. Explicit
element scopes save and restore the segment value through their existing owner.

Pending calls retain stable builder positions, cleanup depth and a paused-region
flag. A branch or jump opens the still-unmarked same-block call interval and
closes it at the edge; the call after its operand join opens no second region.
Closing a region advances pending positions in the same block. The private
builder suffix query distinguishes markers inserted before and after a captured
position without invalidating instruction or result-definition identities.
Argument storage preserves the pending nothrow/hidden start and aggregate-member
context while avoiding a region before a later logical branch's argument address.

The ownership test lives at the driver integration boundary, with a lower-owned
assertion helper: it proves an empty constructor result contributes no result
temporary but its two by-value parameters remain in both full and operand-only
queries, scratch is released, no cleanup becomes live during a query, and syntax
counts remain unchanged. The builder test checks marker intervals and stable
definition identities. The compiler retains one syntax tree and recorded semantic
facts; no lookup, parsing, overload selection or demand is added to lowering.
Every temporary query, mark, segment, cleanup and instruction belongs to its
function. No optimizer, register allocator, native writer, dependency, unsafe,
FFI, subprocess implementation or environment access changed. Frozen fixtures,
references, protected policy and Ralph state were not edited.

else store. Its final protected mismatch is retained by

reopening this enclosing else region; translating that choice traversal is a
separate next dependency. This is an incomplete port path, not an oracle defect
or waived comparison. The other 44 probes match exactly.

## Fourth independent checkpoint audit — 2026-10-15

Audit entry/base: `cd3e03a0f8942cd115795e8fffd0f2d3b444907c`, clean checkout,
**292/361** fixed-manifest passes. Previous last-audited implementation:
`3335e750c36a9a56689943965b3e3b9bf13f82d5`. Reviewed the entire intervening diff,
including accepted checkpoints `91a5020`, `d43110c` and `cd3e03a` and the
preceding documentation-only audit handoff. Reviewed implementation including
these audit fixes: `b2c5224a31d25e826380d011fa1fb6718ac53d82`; the top marker advances
to that implementation. Stage base remains unchanged. This certifies the three
reviewed partial groups, not completion of PA12.

The last repeated stage-only check was **no progress**: the same 292/361 result.
The authoritative clean checkout and accepted implementation commits established
the next safe action, this independent audit. Those accepted groups themselves
made progress (265 → 274 → 289 → 292). This audit fixes completed-scope defects
outside the fixed manifest; unchanged fixture counts do not waive them. Ralph
continues to own the phase goal, gate policy and stage advancement.

### Scope, frozen owners and findings

Read `AGENTS.md`, `CLONE_CONTRACT.md`, `spec.md`, `docs/rust-clone-plan.md`,
`pa12/README.md`, this ledger and `docs/port/source-map.tsv`. Consulted frozen
C++ commit `3438f5aea24d8fcc9a0113316f7e27c890cd5ac6` directly, particularly:

- `sema/analyzer_construction.cpp::{allocation,new_deallocation,deletion,
  usual_deallocation,sized_array_delete}` and
  `sema/analyzer_initialization.cpp::array_new_list`: bound promotion and
  diagnostic precedence, selected allocation/deallocation identity, trailing
  constructor demand, destruction access and cookie requirements.
- `lower/allocation_lowering.cpp::{array_new_shape,count_may_be_invalid,
  length_may_throw,invalid_length,array_allocation,new_list_elements,
  construct_new_elements,array_cookie,free_storage,deletion}`: target-width
  arithmetic, allocation-size retention, flattened count/cookie layout,
  initializer prefixes, constructor attempts, null paths and reverse destruction.
- `lower/aggregate_lowering.cpp::{member_may_throw,omitted_may_throw,
  counts_elements,push_counted,element_made}` and the aggregate path/row owners:
  plain constructor throw queries, completed-prefix ownership and row reuse.
- `lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp::{creates_temporaries,invokes_caller_destroyed,
  caller_destroys,element_temporaries,begin_element_temporaries,
  end_element_temporaries,paused_call_region,before_throwing_call,
  close_region,drop_empty_region,split_region,chain_entry,drop_segment_floor}`,
  `lower/function_lowering.cpp::terminate`, `lower/condition_lowering.cpp` and
  `lower/program_lowering.cpp::resolve_singleton_roles`: function-local
  temporaries, hidden segments, stable insertion positions, cleanup-cache
  invalidation, branch cleanup floors and deterministic runtime forwarding.

Fresh protected-adapter probes ran against the accepted entry before edits.
`initial-probes.json`, `probes-start.log` and the retained mismatch directories
reproduce **six** accepted-checkpoint divergences across **three** defects.
Both tools accepted these shallow inputs; full LowIR bytes differed. The final
59-probe matrix matches every comparison, including all six reproducers:

1. `omitted_aggregate_throws` incorrectly included default-argument evaluation.
   The frozen omitted-element query asks whether the selected nontrivial default
   constructor itself can throw. Its separate construction-attempt query includes
   evaluated default arguments. Rust now preserves that distinction. A listed
   prefix followed by `A(int = next()) noexcept` no longer acquires extra storage
   and counted-prefix cleanup regions. Actual throws while evaluating a trailing
   default still use the constructor-attempt handler.
2. A trailing array-constructor loop omitted the frozen hidden full-expression
   guard when the constructor and its default temporary were nonthrowing.
   Its attempt now includes the existing hidden segment fact independently of
   plain prefix cleanup demand. Explicit element-temporary scopes still own and
   destroy each default's temporaries before the next element; a prefix temporary
   retains its full-expression lifetime. Throwing/nonthrowing constructor and
   destructor variants, nonthrowing allocators, returns, locals and call arguments
   all match the frozen output.
3. Empty ordinary exception regions were emitted as `eh_try`/`eh_end`, prematurely
   placing pending handlers before nested array-length branches. Frozen C++ drops
   the matching empty marker and retains those pending chains; a subsequent region
   reusing the same chains defers their placement. Rust now records the active
   region target, unlinks only that target's trailing marker, rebases captured
   expression/storage/guard/call positions and preserves cleanup reuse/layout.
   Tracked regions remain explicit. The private builder keeps instruction and
   result-definition IDs stable; logical instruction statistics count live block
   instructions rather than discarded marker slots. An invariant test validates
   target matching, predecessor insertion, definition identity and exact counts.

Three required regressions are in PA12's executed `tests/controls/` lane:
`400-array-new-prefix-default-argument-regions.t`,
`400-array-new-hidden-default-temporary-regions.t` and
`400-array-new-nested-storage-regions.t`. All require exit zero and also pass
protected raw stream/LowIR comparison. No existing fixture was formatted or
modified; references, the manifest, checker policy and Ralph state are unchanged.

### Algorithm, ownership and architecture review

Allocation facts retain the original bound `ExprId`, canonical target/prefix
`TypeId`, selected `BindingId`/`ConstructorId`, deallocator and sized-cookie fact.
Bound promotion, constant rejection, list overflow and destruction access stay
in semantics; lowering consumes those facts without repeating lookup or ranking.
A full constant prefix does not demand a default constructor. Unused class
layouts and callable bodies remain undemanded; the existing ownership tests
verify unchanged syntax counts and canonical prefix/element identity. Scratch
argument ranking restores reusable capacity on success and failure.

The array shape flattens only its own recorded dimensions. Target products,
offsets and subtraction use explicit wrapping arithmetic; runtime counts widen
by their recorded promoted signedness. Prefix and PTRDIFF_MAX checks retain
C++'s ordering and diagnostics; cookie alignment and flattened element counts
remain separate from the first dimension. Placement operands evaluate once and
supply the matching deallocation call. Nothrow allocation preserves both invalid
length and returned-null paths. Byte-zeroing, typed field/array projections,
volatile flags, constructor/default evaluation and reverse destruction retain
exact instruction order.

Counted prefix/row state, completion counters, placement Values, constructor
attempts and destructor-unwind guards belong to one `Function`. Element scopes
restore the enclosing segment and insertion state. Full-expression scans read
original expression/category/conversion and declared parameter facts: an empty
constructor destination contributes no destructor, while its by-value parameters
remain caller-owned temporaries. Logical left operands remain alive across the
right branch; only the right operand's local suffix ends there. A nothrow segment's
cleanup floor is invalidated before a throwing call/destructor and at segment end.

Empty-region removal mutates one block's order through the private typed builder;
it does not renumber IDs, clone the body, parse output text or invalidate unrelated
functions. Pending cleanup chains stay function-owned. The two allocator roles
inspect only demanded allocation-symbol records and stream each secondary
forwarder's body before releasing it; all eight multi-unit ordering/replacement
probes match. Semantic identity, body demand and presentation order remain distinct.
The existing O0 sink releases typed bodies after streaming; only typed LowIR and
symbol metadata cross that boundary.

The complete reviewed diff introduces no unsafe/FFI path, C++ implementation
fallback, fixture recognition, dependency, environment/process read, suppressed
Clippy warning, mechanical source split, second parser or whole-program retry.
Production tool spellings remain compatible; Rust names follow Rust conventions.
The new array modules have separate shape/initialization/construction/destruction
responsibilities recorded in the source map. No optimizer, register allocator or
native object owner changed. Templates and source-to-ELF completion remain later
stages; PA34 object provenance/bootstrap is not claimed by this PA12 audit.

### Resource observations and protected stack qualification

After build and gate processes finished, `measure.py` generated fixed supported
100/400/1600-function inputs, **8,188 / 32,788 / 131,788** source bytes. Each
function creates/deletes a class array with a listed default temporary and returns
an aggregate array holding two nested class allocations. All three protected
`scaling.jsonl` receipts match. An oracle A/A calibration and two paired ABBA
blocks retain **30** observations, four measured samples per tool/input. Every
timed run exits zero and its output hash equals the protected receipt.
`resources.json`, `resources-final.log` and `scaling/` retain raw observations,
streams, RSS and output identities.

| Functions | Rust mean ms / peak RSS KiB | C++ mean ms / peak RSS KiB |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| 100 | 44.55 / 8032 | 18.88 / 10060 |
| 400 | 122.47 / 14924 | 50.23 / 12788 |
| 1600 | 443.76 / 44544 | 172.36 / 19188 |

For 16x the function bodies, observed Rust time grows **9.96x** and peak RSS
**5.55x**. These startup-inclusive costs describe this workload, not a universal
complexity proof or a PA35 generated-code target.

`stack.py` uses one fixed **512-parenthesis** array bound at a common initial
**1 MiB** native stack. The protected receipt in `stack.jsonl` has **match=true**
and **stack_qualification.passed=true**: both tools succeed at a common
**16 MiB** with identical streams and complete generated output. Raw initial and
qualified attempts are in
`stack-qualifications/d77dd3ab897e45e49c3a08891c776933/`; `stack-final.log`
retains the receipt. Acceptance reads its qualified `match`, because the adapter
preserves the candidate's initial guard result. No native exhaustion cutoff,
diagnostic position, byte-budget guard or stack policy was calibrated or changed.

## Derived value arguments and selected-copy exception queries — 2026-10-15

Implementation entry/base: `0b22c9f2922be5abf52978e0ccfdd1384541e578`, clean
checkout, **292/361** fixed-manifest passes. Stage base remains
`b9f3dba87a8cc2859ff489ed09f1ddf2cb95dc4f`; last-audited remains
`b2c5224a31d25e826380d011fa1fb6718ac53d82`. This is an unreviewed coherent
partial implementation handoff. Ralph owns checkpoint/final audits and advancement.
The preceding stage-only attempt was **no progress** (292/361 again); inspecting
clean HEAD, the actual failure traces and the audited ownership boundaries
established the next implementation work.

Read the repository guide, clone contract, specification, Rust clone plan,
PA12 handout and ledger. The initial 69 failures mostly stop at conversion-function
semantics. A separate prerequisite group in the same class-value path has four
failures: standard conversion to a base value parameter, empty-class slicing,
and the exception query of an unevaluated implicit copy. Those failures are
completed here; conversion-function discovery/ranking remains unfinished.

### Frozen owners, behavior and typed data flow

Consulted the frozen C++ snapshot directly, especially:

- `sema/conversions.cpp::ConversionRules::{implicit,standard,reference,compare}`:
  class-value identity/derived-to-base sequences and overload ranking.
- `sema/analyzer_construction.cpp::{copy_initialization,record_copy,select_constructor}`
  and `sema/analyzer_copy.cpp::{implicit_copy_members,implicit_copy_member,defaulted_copy_facts}`:
  selected copy/move binding, original source identity and deferred callable demand.
- `sema/analyzer_exceptions.cpp::{noexcept_expression,function_may_throw,conversion_may_throw,may_throw}`:
  unevaluated selected transfers, explicit specifications and evaluated defaults.
- `lower/expression_lowering.cpp::push_converted`,
  `lower/object_lowering.cpp::{call_constructor,initialize_from,temporary_object,zero_object}`,
  `lower/statement_lowering.cpp::class_return` and
  `lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp::{creates_temporaries,begin_full_expression}`:
  source temporary versus destination, base projection, empty zeroing and regions.

Rust `sema/scalar_conversions.rs` now handles class values before scalar lvalue
transformations. An identity sequence has Exact rank; a derived-to-base value
sequence has Conversion rank. Both retain canonical source/target `TypeId`s and
no lvalue-to-rvalue read. Ranking does not select a copy constructor or demand
its body. The chosen call's existing `record_class_copy` selects the base's copy
or move with the original derived value category and retains its reference
binding and checked `BasePathId`. A derived prvalue remains a distinct complete
source object. Its actual selected reference binding prepares destruction/layout
before lowering; own-class elided values keep their prior demand boundary.

`lower/materialization.rs` applies the empty-parameter shortcut only when a
functional source has the parameter's own class. Slicing even an empty derived
prvalue keeps its source storage, construction effects and base projection.
`lower/temporary_scan.rs` includes a differing-class root when returning a class
value: the source temporary is destroyed, including when the base transfer is
trivial storage and the source destructor can throw. Same-class results keep
operand-only scans. `lower/class_results.rs` always consumes the selected copy
action. Function-owned `EmptyValueZeroing::{Emit,Omit}`, restored after the
initialization, preserves the frozen empty functional-return zeroing convention
without constructing the derived object in the base destination.

`sema/throw_queries.rs` consumes a selected operand's `CopyAction`: a constructor
contributes its exception properties and unwritten trailing defaults, and a
trivial declared storage transfer retains an explicit exception specification.
This fixes `noexcept(E(e))` for an implicit nontrivial copy whose member copy can
throw. The query retains its selected facts without requesting emission.

Three semantic invariant tests verify unchanged parsed expression counts,
original source `TypeId`/category and parenthesis aliases, selected copy versus
move `ConstructorId`/reference route, no copy-callable demand from a losing base
value overload, and no emission from an unevaluated implicit-copy query. The
existing unused-default-argument layout invariant remains green. The production
change adds no syntax nodes, textual phase transport, semantic lookup during
lowering, whole-program retry, dependency, unsafe implementation or environment
read. The existing optimizer, register allocator and native generators retain
their owners and behavior.

Two new PA12 executed controls are
`tests/controls/400-derived-value-source-temporary.t` and
`tests/controls/400-unevaluated-copy-exception-properties.t`. They check runtime
copy/move effects, complete-source lifetime, empty derived arguments/returns,
recursive copy/move exception properties and throwing default arguments. Existing
fixtures and protected manifests/checkers/references are unchanged.

## Scalar conversion functions and declared-condition lifetimes

Group base: `4a82ffb7c03bfaa61689138e5c717f79445a1a6d`. The preceding implementation turn is
**progress**: its committed derived-value group and protected evidence establish
292 → 296 fixed passes. This group starts from the clean 296/361 checkpoint;
it does not advance the last-audited commit or the stage.

Evidence root (local generated artifacts, excluded from Git):
`/home/vishvananda/work/private/v5rust/checkout/artifacts/pa12-conversion-functions`.
`base.txt`, `stage-start.json` and `progress-final.json` preserve the entry state.
The existing stage base and last-audited commit above remain authoritative.

### C++/Rust owners and completed behavior

Consulted the frozen C++ owners directly:

- `sema/analyzer_conversions.cpp::{visible_conversions,conversion_set,
  user_conversion,user_candidate_order,apply_user_conversion,
  conversion_subobject}` and `analyzer_expressions.cpp::{convert,
  explicit_conversion}`: discovery, hiding, implicit-object viability,
  first/second standard ranking, selected access/deletion and demand.
- `lower/conversion_lowering.cpp::{conversion_object,call_conversion,
  converted_value,converted_operand}` and `expression_lowering.cpp`: original
  receiver evaluation, scalar reference reads and context-specific conversions.
- `lower/statement_lowering.cpp::{condition_variable,if_statement,
  while_statement,for_statement,switch_statement}`: condition calls, dispatch
  and spills, scope depths and per-iteration destruction.
- `lower/symbol_lowering.cpp::{function_encoding,function_spelling,
  overload_ordinal}`: conversion terminals and declaration-order presentation.

Rust owns the new algorithms in `sema/conversion_functions.rs` and
`lower/conversion_functions.rs`, integrated through `user_conversions.rs`,
`procedural_calls.rs`, `scalar_conversions.rs`, `scalar_casts.rs`,
`procedural_statements.rs`, `expression_facts.rs`, `graph.rs`, `lowering.rs`,
`throw_queries.rs` and `lower/{conditions,statements,expressions,symbols,
throw_scan}.rs`. The source map records each owning C++ responsibility.

Completed the **known non-class scalar target** conversion-function group:
implicit/direct scalar initialization, ordinary and operator call arguments,
scalar returns, static/C-style scalar casts and contextual boolean expressions
and declarations. The nonvirtual complete-class inventory follows base edges
in declaration order with an explicit stack, canonical return-type/template-kind
hiding counts and at most two visits per class. It deduplicates callable
identities while leaving repeated base-subobject ambiguity for selected-use
validation. Inventories are memoized on demand by canonical `EntityId`; no
unrelated declarations or functions are scanned. Source cv/category and member
ref-qualifiers affect viability and first-step ranking; second standard sequences
break conversion-function ties. Explicit scalar conversions permit only the
qualification-only following step. Deletion/access are checked after unique
selection, and only the selected function is demanded.

`UserConversion::{Constructor,ImplicitConstructor,Function}` separates the
callable kinds. A function sequence retains its second standard conversion's
original rank independently of the outer user-defined rank. Sparse original
`ExprId`/`DeclaratorId` records own the selected `BindingId`, canonical result,
value category, retained `BasePathId` and following conversion. Parentheses share
one record; the original AST, source type, category and expression identity stay
unchanged. A typed `MemberCall` view supplies emission directly; lowering never
repeats lookup or overload selection. `FunctionName::Conversion(TypeId)` feeds
the existing ABI graph's conversion node, while original syntax supplies labels
and the canonical declaration inventory supplies overload ordinals.

Normal scalar use reads a reference conversion result before its standard
conversion. The frozen scalar reference-result load uses a typed unqualified
place, including a volatile reference result. Ordinary truth and logical-not
consume the raw conversion result; they do not insert a redundant bool
comparison. Declared conditions retain the frozen direct call-boundary behavior
for reference results. These peculiarities are translated without correcting or
optimizing the C++ behavior.

A declared condition initializes before its conversion. Its throwing conversion
gets the C++ dispatch/return spill/declared-object unwind/continuation sequence.
Selection scopes retain the condition object through both branches and remove
its cleanup at the statement's end, so an outer false branch never inherits an
unconstructed nested object's cleanup. While condition objects are destroyed on
body completion, continue, break and the condition's false edge. For condition
objects survive the iteration expression, then end on retest or exit; for-init
objects survive the whole loop. Cleanup state, spill slots, block identities and
exception chains remain owned by one `Function`. Same/derived-class standard
copy selection now excludes user conversions by its existing identity rule
without rejecting a class merely because it declares unrelated conversions.
The optimizer, allocator and native code generators are unchanged.

## Converted built-in operator group handoff

is **progress**, demonstrated by its committed scalar-conversion and condition
lifetime implementation and **296 → 308** protected fixed passes. This group
began from a clean **308/361** checkout. Stage base and last-audited commit
remain unchanged; implementation does not advance either the audit or PA12.

Evidence root:
`/home/vishvananda/work/private/v5rust/artifacts/pa12-builtin-operators`.
`stage-start.json`, `baseline.json`, `baseline.log`, `base.txt` and
`ledger-before.md` preserve the entry state. The fixed 361-case manifest remains
`c4c44d806ca2a7ba77b579b6d90d6a14134cd192278c2147c40413cc9db74033`.

### Translation and architecture review

Read the frozen `sema/analyzer_operators.cpp::{ConvertedBuiltin,
class_builtin_candidates,builtin_operator,overloaded_operator}` and
`sema/analyzer_conversions.cpp::{user_conversion,convert_class_operand}`
directly. Lowering follows `lower/conversion_lowering.cpp::{call_conversion,
converted_operand,converted_value}` and
`lower/expression_lowering.cpp::{expression,glvalue}`. The main Rust owners
are `sema/{builtin_operators,operator_candidates,operator_resolution,
conversion_functions,scalar_operators,scalar_conversions}.rs` and
`lower/{conversion_functions,expressions,mutations}.rs`; the graph's read-only
views stay in `sema/lowering.rs`. `docs/port/source-map.tsv` records these
responsibilities, including the result-versus-receiver base paths.

For an eligible class operand, the existing complete-class conversion inventory
supplies distinct non-explicit result `TypeId`s. Reference results retain their
lvalue/xvalue categories; arrays and function references supply the oracle's
decayed alternatives. Unresolved overload sets supply no builtin type.
Query-local operand spans share a reusable vector alongside the existing
candidate/sequence buffers. Two inline fact arrays hold original and converted
views. Each required combination computes the best member of its builtin
parameter family: unary arithmetic/indirection, prefix/postfix mutation,
arithmetic/bitwise/shifts, pointer arithmetic/difference, comparisons, subscript
and compound assignment. Parameter lists are deduplicated and viable ordinary
non-template nonmembers suppress the corresponding builtin list. Existing
user-defined conversion sequences and candidate comparison rules decide the
winner, including member/nonmember competition and ambiguous results.

Only the winning conversion function is accessed, demanded and recorded on the
original alias-rooted `ExprId`. A sparse `FunctionConversion` stores its callable,
result/category, receiver `BasePathId`, second standard conversion and separate
result `BasePathId`; dense source facts and original syntax remain unchanged.
The builtin checker borrows an effective result view and records its final
standard step without resolving the call again. Scalar reference-target
selection is exposed only for the builtin reference parameters here; general
reference initialization and class-valued conversion competition remain at
the next boundary. Braced operands bypass converted builtin enumeration and
do not require premature expression facts.

Lowering consumes those selected typed facts. A reference mutation obtains
its referent once, then preserves the normal load/RHS/update/store order.
Binary expressions preserve the C++ ordering of evaluation versus standard
conversion and base projection. The original receiver place is obtained
without reentering its own converted operand. Prvalue receivers use existing
materialization and full-expression cleanup paths. Builtin scalar glvalues
retain volatile/typed load facts; aggregate reference-result loads have no
scalar memory flags. Ordinary scalar conversion initialization retains its
existing unqualified-load convention. Receiver and converted-result base
paths never overwrite one another. All call, temporary, block and cleanup
state remains owned by the current lowered function. No optimizer, allocator,
native emission, byte-budget guard, phase transport or environment boundary
changes; no new dependency, unsafe code or compiler fallback.

Three Rust invariant regressions check retained source/parenthesis identity,
no extra AST allocation, only the selected converter demanded, ambiguity
scratch restoration before another query, and distinct receiver/result base
paths. The pre-existing empty-brace temporary-materialization case caught a
premature fact read during development; the final whole-manifest preservation
check includes that restored identity.

### Reproducible frozen deletion behavior

`probes/178.cpp` reduces a frozen semantic defect:

```cpp
struct X { operator int() = delete; };
void f(X &x) { x + 2; }
```

Invoking the frozen `cppgm++ --emit-lowir -O0 -o result.lowir source.cpp`
succeeds and emits a call to the deleted converter. Its
`convert_class_operand` owner omits the deletion check that
`apply_user_conversion` performs for ordinary initialization. This port
preserves that stable observable result only in the builtin path. The
protected raw before/after receipts and complete outputs are in `probes.jsonl`
and `mismatches/`; `probe-index.json` maps all generated probes to their
purpose. No reference, checker, acceptance exception or fixture was changed.

### Validation, costs and remaining boundary

`stage-final.json` and `progress-final.json` establish **308 → 322** fixed

### Scope and frozen owners

Read `AGENTS.md`, `CLONE_CONTRACT.md`, `spec.md`, `docs/rust-clone-plan.md`,
`pa12/README.md`, this ledger and the source map. Consulted frozen C++ commit
`3438f5aea24d8fcc9a0113316f7e27c890cd5ac6` directly:

- `sema/conversions.cpp` and `analyzer_construction.cpp`: class identity/base
  standard ranking, selected copy/move bindings and original source lifetime.
- `sema/analyzer_conversions.cpp::{visible_conversions,conversion_set,
  user_conversion,user_candidate_order,apply_user_conversion,
  convert_class_operand,convert_to_scalar}`: inventories, hiding, object and
  second-step ranking, selected access/deletion, demand and converted operands.
- `sema/analyzer_operators.cpp::{ConvertedBuiltin,class_builtin_candidates,
  builtin_operator,overloaded_operator}`: complete required type alternatives,
  parameter families, suppression, competition, reference mutations and ordering.
- `sema/analyzer_exceptions.cpp::{function_may_throw,conversion_may_throw,
  temporary_may_throw,may_throw}`: implicit transfers versus written construction
  and terminal converted-operand exception queries.
- `lower/{conversion_lowering,expression_lowering,object_lowering,
  statement_lowering,cleanup_lowering,symbol_lowering}.cpp`: result/receiver
  projections, scalar loads, empty slicing, temporary ownership, condition
  objects, call regions, destruction and canonical symbol/emission order.

Rust owners are `sema/{conversion_functions,builtin_operators,
operator_candidates,operator_resolution,scalar_conversions,copy_initialization,
throw_queries,inheritance,procedural_calls,procedural_statements,
expression_facts,graph,lowering}.rs` and
`lower/{conversion_functions,conditions,expressions,mutations,statements,
class_results,construction,materialization,temporary_scan,throw_scan,symbols}.rs`.
`docs/port/source-map.tsv` records the translated responsibilities and audit fixes.

### Findings and fixes

1. **Lost scalar conversion-result base path.** `operator Derived*()` and
   `operator Derived*&()` followed by conversion to `Base*` omitted the zero-offset
   projection in returns/local initialization. Call arguments instead tried to
   project the original receiver class, producing `internal compiler error:
   retaining a nonunique base path`. Retain the second step's `BasePathId`
   independently of the receiver path; preserve it when recording call arguments;
   lower it after the selected conversion call. The ownership test uses both
   a derived receiver and a derived-pointer reference result, keeps original
   parenthesis/source facts and distinguishes the two path identities.
   Ten public/private/cast probes also preserve the frozen scalar path's omission
   of second-step access checking and its cast-context projection convention.
2. **Incorrect contextual volatile reference-result load.** Logical-not and
   logical truth used ordinary scalar initialization's unqualified load. C++ uses
   `converted_operand`'s qualified glvalue load. A shared conversion-call helper
   now accepts the consuming path's load policy; contextual truth preserves
   volatile loads and ordinary initialization retains its frozen typed loads.
   Calls, spills, destruction and evaluation count retain their existing order.
3. **Implicit-copy exception queries counted extra defaults.** A selected implicit
   conversion's `CopyAction` called the full written-construction query. Frozen
   `conversion_may_throw` only queries its selected callable, so a noexcept base
   copy with a throwing trailing default still makes `noexcept(take(derived))`
   true there. Query only callable properties for that conversion. Written
   `Base(derived)` separately queries the copy and defaults through its retained
   `ClassInitialization::FromExpression` source use. No second selection or body
   demand is introduced. The existing throwing-default construction control stays
   passing. This reproduces the frozen query distinction without altering runtime
   default-argument evaluation.
4. **Converted temporary exception queries revisited receiver construction.**
   Frozen `may_throw` treats a converted builtin/contextual operand as terminal:
   the converter and original prvalue's destructor count, but construction and
   children of the original receiver are not walked. Rust instead walked its
   constructor, changing the folded result. Typed
   `ConversionUse::{Scalar,Contextual,Builtin}` now retains this distinction.
   Ordinary scalar initialization keeps its original-expression query. Tests
   check both folded results and no body emission or AST growth. The cohesive
   class-temporary query helper keeps strict Clippy's 240-line limit without
   suppression, macro extraction or mechanical file splitting.
5. **Unevaluated projections requested physical layout.** Retaining the result
   route initially requested storage layout even inside `noexcept`. Base-path
   checking now requests layout only for evaluated lowering. The canonical path
   remains available for semantic checking. A unit invariant checks that the
   result classes stay `LayoutState::NotStarted`, syntax does not grow and no
   trailing emission demand appears. Evaluated base projections retain their
   ordinary physical-layout demand.

Four required regressions are in PA12's existing executed controls lane:

- `400-scalar-conversion-result-base-path.t`
- `400-contextual-volatile-conversion-result.t`
- `400-selected-copy-noexcept-query-boundary.t`
- `400-converted-temporary-noexcept-query.t`

All four plus the existing `400-unevaluated-copy-exception-properties.t` pass
course runtime predicates and protected exact invocation comparison;
`regressions-final.json` retains their course logs and trace paths. No frozen
fixture, reference sidecar or protected acceptance rule changed.

### Algorithm, ownership and failure-path review

Complete-class inventories are memoized by canonical class identity and follow
only declared conversion/base edges. Hiding counts follow the active path;
callable deduplication and bounded repeated-base visits preserve ambiguity.
Incomplete/template/using inventories are explicitly still pending. Candidates
are ranked without demanding losing converter bodies. Builtin type alternatives
retain reference categories and decayed arrays/functions, exhaust required
operand combinations, deduplicate parameter families and suppress viable ordinary
nonmembers' equivalent families. Query-local scratch is restored on ambiguity
and error, as the retained rollback regression independently checks.

Selections retain compact callable/type/path IDs on original expression aliases;
ordinary, contextual and builtin uses are distinct typed facts. Lowering consumes
those facts without lookup, reparsing or tree cloning. Derived prvalue slicing
retains complete source storage, constructor effects and destruction even for
empty bases. Selected copy queries reuse constructor properties and do not emit
unevaluated bodies. Declared conditions keep objects alive through both branches
or the complete loop iteration; false edges, continue, break and throwing
conversions use the proper cleanup depth. The independent condition matrix
compares all these emitted cleanup paths to C++.

Function-owned call, branch, temporary and cleanup storage is released at its
existing boundary. Unit-owned inventories, semantic facts and compact ABI symbols
are shared until their last consumer. Output order follows declaration/candidate
vectors and existing demand/definition scheduling, never hash iteration. Numeric
probes cover bool, narrow signed/unsigned and volatile references, 32/64-bit
constants, float/double/extended results, shifts, compound updates, pointer/null
and function-pointer alternatives, including regular rejections. Exact artifacts,
diagnostic bytes and ordering are checked by the protected adapter.

No new dependency, unsafe path, suppressed lint, fixture recognition, compiler
fallback, duplicate parser, whole-program retry or environment/process read is
introduced. Optimizer, register allocator and native generation are unchanged.
The PA12 course backend is the permitted frozen evaluator. This audit makes no
PA34 object/bootstrap claim or future template/class-result conversion claim.

## Unique scalar conversion consumers — implementation handoff, 2026-10-15

Entry was clean `6abca79121bb4f442081710baba2d39c54692781`, with
**322/361** fixed identities passing and **39** failing. The stage base remains
`b9f3dba87a8cc2859ff489ed09f1ddf2cb95dc4f`; the last-audited implementation remains
`2bcb505bb59621850d8c43e8cf377780f9e6a842`. This implementation does not advance
that marker or the assignment. The preceding audit was progress through retained
validation and a concrete next boundary, without a fixed-case gain. This group
implements that next boundary for completed ordinary classes.

### C++ owners, divergence and completed behavior

Consulted the frozen `3438f5aea24d8fcc9a0113316f7e27c890cd5ac6` implementation:

- `sema/analyzer_conversions.cpp::{conversion_functions,visible_conversions,
  unique_conversion,convert_class_operand}`: visible declaration inventories,
  result predicates, declaration uniqueness, original receiver and selected call.
- `sema/analyzer_bodies.cpp::switch_condition`: expression discovery versus
  declaration discovery followed by ordinary overload selection and promotion.
- `sema/analyzer_construction.cpp::{allocation,deletion}`: integral/unscoped-enum
  bounds and non-void object-pointer operands before existing storage/lifetime work.
- `sema/analyzer_constexpr.cpp::integral_constant_operand`: inspected the related
  constant-expression consumer; actual constexpr call interpretation is a separate
  remaining dependency, not implemented by this group.
- `lower/{conversion_lowering,statement_lowering,allocation_lowering}.cpp`:
  qualified reference-result reads, declared condition boundary conversions,
  count checks, cookies, destruction, deallocation and call cleanup ordering.

The earliest divergence in the four gained cases was semantic target discovery:
Rust rejected class switches and returned 86 for class bounds/deletion. A typed
`ConversionTarget` now filters the existing complete-class conversion inventory by
integral/unscoped-enum, integral/all-enum or non-void object-pointer result. A result
reference is removed before the predicate; explicit functions and templates do
not count. Counting visible declarations happens before object cv/ref viability:
two same-target ref-qualified declarations are still nonunique. Existing inherited
hiding, function deduplication and repeated-subobject diagnostics remain in force.
Unique expression consumers preserve the frozen omission of deletion and receiver
viability checks, while checking selected member/base access. A switch declaration
instead discovers its target and then runs ordinary overload selection, including
its normal deletion and viability checks. No new optimization is introduced.

Rust owners are `sema/{conversion_functions,procedural_statements,array_allocation,
allocation}.rs` and `lower/conditions.rs`, with existing expression conversion,
array/deletion lowering and per-function lifetime owners. The source map records
these responsibilities. The graph keeps original ExprId aliases, class type and
value category, then records one callable, result category, receiver BasePathId and
final standard conversion. Declarations keep the selected call on DeclaratorId.
Lowering consumes these facts, with qualified loads of expression reference results,
without lookup, reselection, reparsing, synthetic nodes or tree copies.

The inventory is memoized only for its complete canonical class identity; walks
follow declared base edges in deterministic order, with the existing bounded
repeated-base visits. Each discovery scans only that inventory and allocates no
candidate collection. Ordinary switch declaration ranking retains existing
query-local overload scratch. Shared receiver validation demands only the selected
body and physical base layout only for evaluated lowering. The new ownership test
checks unchanged syntax counts, aliases, original receiver facts, independent
selection identities, final operand promotions, selected-only body demand and an
unrelated class whose layout stays unstarted. Per-function temporary, unwind and
backend storage ownership is unchanged. There is no dependency, unsafe path,
compiler fallback, environment/process read, textual phase transport or optimizer,
register allocator or native-generation change.

Four new required controls in PA12's existing executed lane cover combined runtime
conversion counts, volatile reference loads, switch-object lifetime, pointer-result
loads and single/array destruction; and three rejections for declaration uniqueness,
explicit hiding and multiple object-pointer targets. `regressions-final.json`
records **4/4** successful owning course checks plus protected byte comparisons.
The fixed manifest is unchanged. The fixed-only CLI correctly refused their new
IDs during an initial focused attempt; the unmodified owning `execute_case` adapter
and full course target subsequently executed them. No protected fixture or policy
was edited.

### Oracle defect retained explicitly

`declared-reference-switch.cpp` is a minimal standalone reproducer:

```cpp
struct X { X(); operator int&(); };
void f() { switch (X x = X()) { default: break; } }
```

The frozen C++ compiler exits 0 and emits `convert zext i32 ptr`, converting the
reference boundary directly without loading it. Its own `lowir` validator exits 1
with `invalid conversion widths or categories`. The Rust output preserves those
exact frozen bytes; this is raw parity, not a valid-LowIR or runtime success claim.
`oracle-defect.py`, `oracle-defect.json`, `oracle-defect.jsonl`,
`declared-reference-switch.lowir` and `oracle-reference-validation.log` retain
commands, exits, streams and the reproducer. No baseline, checker or reference was
changed. Independent valid switch, bound and delete work continued. The previously
reported noexcept defaulted-copy oracle defect also remains unresolved.

## Conditional constructor, reference and scalar conversion handoff

Group base: `1e2ffb138887fdb7b431684b0305413894b77512` (the exact base is also
in `artifacts/pa12-conditional-conversions/base.txt`). Stage base and last audited
commit at the top remain unchanged. The preceding goal turn is **progress**:
its committed unique scalar consumer group reduced 39 failures to 35 without
losing passing identities. This implementation group reduces **35 → 31** failures,
retaining all **326** entry passing identities. PA12 remains partial; Ralph owns
checkpoint/final audit and advancement.

### Source responsibilities and typed flow

Consulted the frozen C++ owners directly:

- `sema/analyzer_conversions.cpp::{conditional,conditional_match,
  converted_conditional,builtin_conditional,user_conversion,
  direct_reference_conversion,user_candidate_order}`: the two directional
  matching probes, direct reference-result priority, constructor/function
  candidate ranking, scalar alternatives and selected application.
- `sema/conversions.cpp::compare`: first/second standard sequence ordering and
  indistinguishability of conversions through different functions.
- `lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp::creates_temporaries` and
  `lower/tracked_lifetime_lowering.cpp::{operand_temporaries,
  guarded_temporaries}`: exclude the conditional destination's class object,
  while retaining an original source prvalue of a different class.
- `lower/conversion_lowering.cpp` and `lower/object_lowering.cpp`: existing
  conversion calls, branch destinations and final-copy dependency boundary.

Rust owners are `sema/{class_conditionals,user_conversions,
conversion_functions,builtin_operators}.rs` and
`lower/{temporary_scan,tracked_lifetimes}.rs`, with existing conversion-call,
reference, materialization, copy and branch-lowering owners. The source map now
records the translated responsibilities.

Conditional matching now tries direct standard glvalue binding, an appropriate
reference-returning conversion function, related-class prvalue conversion, then
implicit value conversion, in the frozen order. Failed reference binding still
allows a converting constructor. Both directions are probed without applying
anything; bidirectional viability reports the exact ambiguity. Related classes
retain their standard-only value rule. Same-class prvalues keep the more
cv-qualified result. A selected constructor initializes the common branch
object, and only its other operand receives a new copy action.

`UserCandidate` retains a `BindingId`, first standard `Conversion` and optional
second standard `Conversion`. Constructor candidates use only a standard first
step; function candidates use the original object's cv/ref category and one
standard result step. Direct lvalue-reference results are tried before temporary
construction. Ranking uses the first sequences, then two functions' second
sequences. The reusable user-conversion scratch is restored and cleared on every
success, ambiguity or error. Only selection application validates access/deletion
and demands the chosen body. General initialization's still-partial selection
entry remains distinct from the conditional query that considers both candidate
groups; this handoff does not claim that general class initialization is complete.

When neither operand matches the other, the built-in conditional candidates use
visible non-explicit nonclass results, reference removal, decay, promotions,
composite pointer types and scoped-enum identity. Candidate pairs are deduplicated
by canonical parameter identities. A unique best pair records its conversions on
the original `ExprId` roots. Final arithmetic/pointer conversion is retained.
The pair product and candidate comparisons follow C++'s required candidate space;
there is no scan of unrelated classes or functions. Options and candidate vectors
are owned by one query and released in bulk. Complete conversion inventories stay
memoized by canonical completed class identity.

Original expression type/category, parentheses aliases and source identities
remain intact. Class reference results use the existing sparse `FunctionConversion`
and conditional `BasePathId` records. Lowering consumes those selected facts and
receiver/result paths without lookup, reselection, source reparsing, synthetic
nodes or a semantic tree copy. A destination-aware branch-temporary query now
counts the original source object when its class differs from the destination;
this preserves guard flags, reset order, full-expression lifetime and unwind
cleanup bytes in the two larger gained cases. Its work vector belongs to one
function, and the new destination-specific query is not put into the ordinary
expression-only memo. Per-function IR/backend ownership and emission order are
unchanged. No optimization, allocator, ABI, target arithmetic, native stack budget,
dependency, environment read, process access, unsafe code or fallback was added.

The semantic ownership test checks unchanged syntax/declaration counts, original
operand categories and aliases, separate selected constructor/function facts,
selected-only body/constructor demand, and an unrelated class with unstarted
layout and copy facts. Three new controls in the existing PA12 executed lane
check scalar conversion laziness and receiver destruction, inherited lvalue and
xvalue reference identity/base projection, and constructor/function ambiguity.
All three pass their course checks and protected byte comparisons;
`regressions-final.json` retains their exact paths. They supplement the unchanged
fixed manifest. During development, an assertion in the new scalar control was
moved after its initializer full-expression so it observes destruction at the
correct point; no frozen fixture was changed.

## Ordinary initialization selection and reference binding handoff

Group base: `6511ecadd07c64d582b521c179c9c91c9b59c93a`, recorded on entry
in `artifacts/pa12-initialization-selection/base.txt`. Stage base and last audited
commit at the top remain unchanged. The preceding goal turn is **progress**:
its committed conditional conversion group reduced 35 fixed failures to 31,
with no lost passing identities. This group reduces **31 → 19** failures and
retains all **330** entry passes. PA12 is partial; this is a coherent selection
and reference-binding handoff for Ralph's checkpoint audit, not stage advancement.

### Oracle owners and production invariants

Read `AGENTS.md`, `CLONE_CONTRACT.md`, `spec.md`, `pa12/README.md`,
`docs/rust-clone-plan.md` and this ledger. Consulted frozen C++ directly:

- `sema/analyzer_conversions.cpp::{user_conversion,direct_reference_conversion,
  user_candidate_order,apply_user_conversion}`: competing constructor/function
  groups, reference-result priority, cv/ref first sequences, second sequences,
  selection before access/deletion and the deferred final-copy boundary.
- `sema/analyzer_construction.cpp::{initialize_class,select_constructor,
  direct_initialization}`: same-class precedence, ordinary copy initialization,
  declaration versus expression diagnostic locations and standard-only first
  arguments of converting constructors.
- `sema/analyzer_calls.cpp::{implicit_sequence,object_sequence}`: overload
  designation/list ordering, standard-only rejection before a second user
  conversion, and the original receiver's category and qualification.
- `lower/conversion_lowering.cpp::{call_conversion,converted_reference}`:
  reference result addresses, independent result base paths, and scalar-result
  tmpref storage, conversions and persistence.

Rust owners are `sema/{user_conversions,conversion_functions,
constructor_initialization,procedural_calls}.rs` and
`lower/{conversion_functions,references}.rs`. The source map records these
responsibilities. Ordinary arguments, returns, scalar/reference bindings and
class copy initialization now use the same combined user-candidate query as
conditionals. A source's visible conversion inventory includes hidden-by-derived
and explicitness behavior already owned by its completed-class memo. A direct
lvalue-reference result is probed before constructing a temporary. Otherwise,
constructors and functions compete by first standard sequences; two functions
also compare their result sequences. No group wins merely by being searched first.
Ambiguity returns no sequence; a selected deleted/private function is checked
only after ranking. Class versus aggregate-member initialization retains the
frozen distinct diagnostic and its owning location.

The selected constructor retains its `BindingId` and standard first `Conversion`
in `ArgumentSequence`; `prepare_converting_constructor` places that step into
reused call scratch without re-running selection. The normal default checks,
access checks, argument application and constructor demand consume it.
`ArgumentConversions::{Standard,UserDefined}` gives constructor ranking an
explicit allowance. A converting constructor's source argument cannot run a
second user conversion. Overloaded-function designation and list arguments keep
their existing earlier rules. `check_constructor_parameters` owns the selected
signature's parameter destruction, copies, base paths and list narrowing; it is
a semantic responsibility separated from candidate selection.

Reference lowering reads the selected `FunctionConversion`, receiver path,
result type/category and result path on the original `ExprId`. A reference

## Sixth independent partial-checkpoint audit — 2026-10-15

Entry was clean `12e36d521830ea6eeacf2b4104d7ff7a1bb3546a`, with **342/361**
fixed identities passing and **19** failing. The preceding accepted initialization
checkpoint is **progress**, evidenced by its committed selection work and retained
330 → 342 comparison. This audit reviews the complete implementation diff since
`2bcb505bb59621850d8c43e8cf377780f9e6a842`, including the three accepted checkpoints
`1e2ffb138887fdb7b431684b0305413894b77512`,
`6511ecadd07c64d582b521c179c9c91c9b59c93a` and
`12e36d521830ea6eeacf2b4104d7ff7a1bb3546a`, plus the fixes in reviewed implementation
`d02f6628e3ee4c4f788d1696c1e87ca1019ffe79`. The stage base remains
`b9f3dba87a8cc2859ff489ed09f1ddf2cb95dc4f`. The last-audited marker now names that
reviewed implementation, rather than this ledger-only commit. Ralph retains goal,
gate and advancement ownership; its state was not edited.

### Reviewed responsibilities and architecture

Read `AGENTS.md`, `CLONE_CONTRACT.md`, `spec.md`, `docs/rust-clone-plan.md`,
`pa12/README.md`, this ledger and the Rust source map. Consulted the frozen C++
`3438f5aea24d8fcc9a0113316f7e27c890cd5ac6` owners directly:

- `sema/analyzer_conversions.cpp`: visible inventories, unique target discovery,
  direct reference priority, constructor/function candidate competition and
  first/second standard sequence ranking, conditional directional matching,
  built-in candidates and selected application/access/deletion ordering.
- `sema/analyzer_construction.cpp`, `analyzer_bodies.cpp::switch_condition` and
  `analyzer_calls.cpp`: ordinary copy initialization, standard-only constructor
  arguments, switch target discovery, array bounds, deletion and object sequences.
- `lower/conversion_lowering.cpp`, `cleanup_lowering.cpp` and
  `tracked_lifetime_lowering.cpp`: reference versus scalar results, receiver/result
  projections, condition calls, temporary discovery, guarded branches and unwind.
- `lower/expression_lowering.cpp::extension_path`,
  `statement_lowering.cpp::local_initializer`,
  `symbol_lowering.cpp::{entry_symbol,function_metadata}` and
  `program_lowering.cpp::emit_definition`: reference ownership, declaration
  metadata, definition retention and complete/base aliases.

Rust review covered `sema/{conversion_functions,user_conversions,
class_conditionals,builtin_operators,constructor_initialization,procedural_calls,
procedural_statements,array_allocation,allocation}.rs` and
`lower/{conversion_functions,conditions,references,temporary_scan,
tracked_lifetimes,function,reference_lifetimes}.rs`, including their typed graph,
materialization, cleanup and symbol interfaces. The source map records both fixes.

The completed groups are ordinary complete-class unique scalar consumers;
constructor/direct-reference/scalar conditional conversions and their source
lifetimes; and ordinary constructor/function selection with scalar or directly
bound reference application. Class-value conversion-function application, explicit
direct-class/list initialization and the other boundaries below remain partial.
Selection fidelity was reviewed separately from those future application paths.

Canonical `TypeId`, `BindingId`, `ConstructorId`, `BasePathId` and original
`ExprId` records carry source cv/category, selected function, first/second
conversions and independent receiver/result projections. No syntax is rewritten or
reparsed; parentheses share the original fact identity. Unique consumers count the
visible declarations before receiver viability, preserving the C++ expression-path
omissions and switch-declaration distinction. Related-class conditionals retain
standard-only rules; unrelated directions are probed before either is applied.
Built-in conditional parameter pairs are deduplicated and must have one best pair.
Constructors and functions compete without preferring a candidate group; direct
lvalue-reference binding precedes temporary construction. Access/deletion and body
demand happen only after selection. Constructor first steps cannot chain another
user conversion; overloaded designations, defaults and list narrowing retain their
own earlier rules and locations.

Complete-class inventories are memoized by canonical class identity and walk only
recorded base/declaration edges in stable order. Hash containers do not determine
emission order. Candidate and temporary-walk scratch is query/function-owned;
taken reusable scratch is cleared and restored on success, ambiguity and error.
Failure paths retain structured errors and explicit status 86 at unsupported
application boundaries. Rejected candidates do not demand bodies or layouts.
Existing ownership tests check unchanged syntax/declaration counts, aliases,
selected-only demand, repeated scratch reuse and unrelated unprepared classes.
No new global scan, needless semantic-tree clone, duplicate parser, fixture
recognition, oracle delegation, dependency, unsafe path, lint suppression or
mechanical file split was found. Production CLI spellings and Rust naming remain
compatible. Per-function temporary/IR storage is released through the existing
owners; optimizer, register allocator and native owners were not changed.

Target-width conversion, signed/unsigned and floating result storage, promotions,
qualified reference loads, array size checks, diagnostic bytes and instruction,
cleanup, declaration and cross-unit emission order were compared through the
protected adapter. The changes do not alter target arithmetic or stack budgets.

### Findings and fixes

1. **Internal constructor declarations carried definition-only retention.** The
   preceding handoff's anonymous-unit probe was a real mismatch: Rust added
   `object_root=yes` to unresolved complete constructor declarations. C++ assigns
   its internal complete-entry retention in `emit_definition`, while pending
   declarations use `function_metadata`. `lower/function.rs::declare` now removes
   the entry's definition-only flag for both constructor variants. Emitted
   complete/base definitions and alias retention are preserved. The original
   four cross-unit probes now match **4/4**. The new executed control
   `tests/controls/500-internal-converting-constructor-declaration.t` failed raw
   comparison before the fix despite course success, and now passes both checks.
2. **Reference-result conversion extended its receiver's lifetime.** An independent
   probe binding `const B&` through a conversion function on a conditional `A`
   prvalue destroyed `A` after the cleanup region instead of at the initializer's
   full-expression. A runtime reproducer also observed the receiver still alive
   after the declaration. The extension-path query inspected original source
   facts without stopping at the selected conversion call. Following C++
   `extension_path`, `lower/reference_lifetimes.rs` now stops at that typed call:
   the bound result owns no lifetime extension of its receiver. The original
   prvalue remains an ordinary full-expression temporary, including its unwind
   cleanup. `tests/controls/500-conversion-reference-receiver-lifetime.t` checks
   both conditional branches and direct prvalues, reference identity and
   destruction before the following statement. It failed course/runtime and raw
   comparison before the fix; it now passes both. All **61/61** independent
   lifetime/count probes match after the fix (previously **60/61**).

Both regressions supplement PA12's existing executed control lane; no frozen
fixture, reference, gate policy or manifest was modified or formatted.
`regression-entry.json`, `regression-entry-trace.jsonl`,
`lifetime-regression-entry.json`, `lifetime-regression-entry-trace.jsonl`,
`regressions-final.json` and retained mismatch outputs establish the before/after
observations. These findings were fixed even though the fixed-case count stayed
unchanged. There is no unresolved finding in the reviewed completed groups.
