## Named conversion-function lookup, imports and definitions handoff

This coherent group starts from clean commit
`9e04a5d0ec5e2063b0aa715dff8a423ddf67966d`, **342/361** fixed-manifest passes and **19** failures. The preceding
goal turn is **progress**: its committed audit fixed constructor declaration
metadata and conversion receiver lifetimes and supplied authoritative evidence,
but left the fixed failure count unchanged, so it did not satisfy stageProgress.
This implementation preserves that work and reduces the same frozen manifest
to **345/361**, **16** failures, with **zero lost passing identities**.
Stage base and last-audited commit above are unchanged; this is an implementation
handoff, not independent certification or stage advancement.

### C++ owners and completed behavior

Read directly in `/home/vishvananda/work/references/v5rust-cpp/dev/src/`:

- `sema/analyzer_conversions.cpp::{conversion_lookup,conversion_set,
  visible_conversions}`: canonical result-type selection after path hiding;
  declaration-scope ambiguity; ordinary receiver overload resolution.
- `sema/analyzer_expressions.cpp::{conversion_name,conversion_member,
  binding_expression,address_of,explicit_conversion}` and
  `sema/analyzer_member_pointers.cpp::member_subobject`: named conversion
  designations, calling-scope target lookup, naming-class/receiver separation,
  base-subobject checks, access/deletion ordering and cast diagnostic locations.
- `sema/analyzer_types.cpp::name_prefix`,
  `sema/analyzer.cpp::{using_declaration,introduce}` and
  `sema/analyzer_declarations.cpp::{special_member,declare_function}`:
  qualified declaration environments, access on qualifier names, shared-name
  using lookup before result filtering, all-import access checks and signature
  hiding in either declaration order.
- `lower/program_lowering.cpp::declaration` and
  `lower/symbol_lowering.cpp::overload_ordinal`: source-defined non-inline
  conversion functions use ordinary function entries; ordinal numbering
  excludes imported functions whose declaration owner is another class.

Rust owners are `sema/{conversion_functions,lookup,member_expressions,
scalar_expressions,function_resolution,function_names,declarations,special_members,
graph,lowering}.rs` and `lower/mod.rs`. The source map records each responsibility.

Explicit `operator T` calls now name functions by canonical result `TypeId`,
including aliases and qualified/reference result types. Unqualified/qualified
names, dot/arrow receivers and qualified addresses retain the original syntax
and feed typed `BindingId` candidate sets to the existing overload resolver.
The target type is looked up in the calling scope. Explicit declarations remain
eligible when named; cv/ref viability and hiding still precede selection.
Missing target functions and declaration-scope ambiguity retain exact diagnostics.
The initial unqualified name of a conversion qualifier is access-checked before
receiver conversion; a qualified implicit receiver retains its naming class
separately from its actual class and selected member's owner. This preserves
both zero-offset projections through an intermediate class.

Conversion using-declarations first search the generic typed conversion name,
then filter the selected scope's result by canonical target. Every imported
access is checked before signature hiding. Imports retain the base entity,
receiver and function origin, while their binding takes the using-declaration's
access. Own identical signatures hide imports written before or after them;
other cv/ref overloads survive. The frozen ordinary introduction path also
accepts conversion using-declarations outside a class; this acceptance is
preserved without manufacturing a class inventory. Templates and incomplete
conversion inventories remain explicit later boundaries, not normal rejections.

Source-defined non-inline conversion bodies now emit at their definition through
an ordinary typed function entry, without requesting a constructor plan. Inline
conversion bodies remain demand-only. The first full course run exposed a raw
helper-name mismatch in the new using control: an imported cv overload shifted
`DeclaredAfter__operatorint` to `DeclaredAfter__operatorint__ov2`. The lowering
view now excludes imports before assigning ordinals, following C++ exactly;
`ordinal-regression-entry/mismatch/` retains the failing raw output pair.

### Typed data flow and architecture review

Canonical result identities, original `ExprId`/`Name`/type syntax and existing
class-scoped visible inventories remain unit-owned. The new shared
`SpecialName::Conversion` key is a private lookup category; it never replaces a
function's canonical `MemberKey` or becomes a rendered semantic identity.
Imports and removals update only the owning scope's indexes and conversion
inventory. They do not change base declarations, published entity ownership or
function origins. Complete-class conversion inventories reuse the existing
unit-lifetime cache; ordinary body queries occur after class completion.

A designation retains receiver type/category and naming class, then the chosen
call records the physical routes once. Lowering consumes those facts directly;
it never repeats lookup, selects another conversion or reparses type spellings.
The ownership regression checks unchanged syntax/declaration counts, selected
own identity, surviving imported overload and its original protected binding,
public import access, filtered own ordinal inventory and absence of losing-body
demand. It also exercises target-directed qualified address selection without
claiming member-pointer result lowering is implemented.

Per-function LowIR streaming and cleanup ownership are unchanged. No optimizer,
register allocator or native implementation was modified; no new optimization,
unsafe code, dependency, environment read, subprocess fallback, fixture
recognition, textual phase transport or whole-program scan was introduced.

## Conversion-result construction, binding and cleanup handoff

This group starts from clean commit
`f6636f3090bf929ae3713e8f20b697df0a2c9e9d`, **345/361** fixed cases and
**16** failures. The preceding named-conversion goal turn is **progress**:
its committed implementation reduced 19 failures to 16 and preserved prior
identities. This group reduces the same frozen manifest to **350/361**,
**11** failures, with **zero lost passing identities**. The stage base and
last-audited commit at the top remain unchanged. This records a coherent
implementation checkpoint; it does not certify full PA12 or advance Ralph.

### Frozen owners and completed behavior

Consulted directly in `/home/vishvananda/work/references/v5rust-cpp/dev/src/`:

- `sema/analyzer_conversions.cpp::{apply_user_conversion,operand_constructor}`:
  a same-class prvalue initializes its destination directly, while reference
  results and derived values select their final direct constructor using only
  standard argument conversions. Unsupported class-value constructor parameters,
  failed selection, deletion and access retain the frozen diagnostic order.
- `sema/analyzer_exceptions.cpp::conversion_may_throw`: the selected final
  constructor participates in exception queries even when its call lowers to
  a storage transfer; its defaults do not become a second conversion query.
- `lower/conversion_lowering.cpp::{call_conversion,conversion_temporary,
  converted_reference}`: destination-aware direct and indirect result calls,
  reference results, complete result temporaries and persistent storage.
- `lower/object_lowering.cpp::{initialize_from,construct_from,elides_conversion,
  local_object}`: selected final transfers, derived-result readdressing before
  base projection, implicit same-class conversion-result copy/move elision,
  explicit conversions retaining their temporary, and the unused `argobj`
  staging slot planned for aggregate locals initialized by direct results.
- `lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp::{creates_temporaries,may_throw,temporary_region,
  open_deferred_region,temporary_done}`, `lower/statement_lowering.cpp::class_return`
  and `lower/lexical_cleanup_lowering.cpp::{lexical_handler,place_lexical_block}`:
  result/receiver lifetime separation, deferred region opening after a result's
  address when earlier objects are alive, and destruction of a completed return
  result before lexical unwinding when a local destructor throws (CWG 2176).

Rust owners are `sema/{conversion_functions,copy_initialization,lowering,
throw_queries}.rs` and `lower/{conversion_functions,construction,materialization,
aggregates,class_results,function,reference_lifetimes,temporary_scan,throw_scan,
lexical_cleanup,lifetime}.rs`. The source map records the translated responsibilities.

Ordinary class-result conversions now initialize locals, by-value arguments,
returns and reference temporaries. The final constructor keeps the conversion
result's cv/category and selected canonical identity. A derived result is a
complete temporary with its own destructor; its base projection supplies the
selected copy/move, and destruction follows that transfer. A same-class prvalue
writes directly to the destination through either ABI boundary. Lowering elides
a selected copy/move fed by an implicit conversion's same-class value; explicit
conversions and ordinary converting constructors retain their distinct rules.
A local reference extends the result's lifetime independently of the receiver.

### Typed ownership and architecture review

The sparse conversion record on the original root `ExprId` owns an optional
`ResultCopy`: the selected `CopySelection` and its lowering action, plus the
retained base route. The canonical result `TypeId`, category, converter binding
and original receiver facts remain separate. In-place results demand no final
copy callable; selected transfers reuse existing constructor-property and body
memos. By-value consumers do not select another constructor from the receiver.
The ownership test verifies unchanged syntax/declaration counts, original
receiver type/category, shared selected copy identity, retained projection and
absence of unrelated callable demand. Signature ABI classification may still
request class properties without requesting a body.

Lowering consumes these facts directly, using ordinary result boundaries and
per-function lifetime storage. `InitializationContext` scopes return receiver
value-initialization policy and restores it on errors. `Lexical::returned` is a
per-function completed-result cleanup; each dispatch snapshots it, shares only
its existing lexical tail, and destroys the result before entering that tail.
Named return-slot ownership retains its separate early-return path. No result
cleanup is activated before initialization succeeds. Ordinary initialization
conversions remain distinct from rewritten builtin operands in temporary scans.

No parser replay, synthesized syntax, rendered identity, semantic lookup during
lowering, speculative whole-program demand or global cache was added. No native
code generator, optimizer or register allocator changed; there is no new
optimization, dependency, unsafe code, environment read or subprocess fallback.

constructor_initialization,user_conversions,procedural_calls,class_casts,

Named conversion calls preserve the original syntax and canonical result
`TypeId`. Indexed class inventories hold `BindingId` imports separately from
function `EntityId` ownership; access and hiding do not alter a base entity.
Complete-class conversion caches follow explicit base edges, with stable
traversal/output ordering. Qualified receiver and naming-class routes are
recorded before lowering. Definition emission and overload ordinals use the
function's original owner; losing overload bodies remain undemanded.

Constructor contexts distinguish copy, direct, copy-list and standard-only
queries. Scratch contains complete `ArgumentSequence` values until selection,
is cleared/restored on success or failure, and publishes compact selected
identities only. The existing ownership regressions check syntax-store marks,
unchanged declaration counts, reusable empty scratch, surviving import/access
identity, undemanded losing/unrelated bodies, selected transfer identity and
bounded dense expression-fact size. Result transfers keep result category and
base projection separate from receiver facts. Same-class prvalues use the
known destination; reference/derived results retain the selected final transfer.

All new lowering state belongs to one `Function`. Error paths restore scoped
zeroing/context settings before propagating their result; terminal lowering
errors discard the function owner. No cleanup is activated before construction
succeeds. Return-result destruction remains separate from named-return-slot
ownership, and dispatches snapshot the completed result before lexical unwind.
The audit's temporary-destruction walk retains only later non-temporary cleanup
actions; it does not clone the full cleanup vector or repeatedly scan a suffix
just to discover those actions. Each required handler consumes typed actions
and the existing prefix chain in deterministic order.

No fixture recognition, C++ implementation delegation, unsafe code, dependency,
new lint suppression, mechanical file split, second parser, textual transport,
rendered semantic identity, unrelated global scan or environment/process read
was introduced. Numeric conversion and layout operations continue through the
existing target-width owners. Diagnostic tests compare exact exit/streams;
output tests compare every emitted byte, including region and helper order.
Optimizer, allocator, native implementation and production tool names are
unchanged. Rust identifiers retain Rust naming conventions.

### Findings and fixes

1. **Scalar allocation completion region omitted for reference conversion
   arguments.** `new A(source)` with `operator A&`, `A&&`, `const A&` or a
   derived reference left no new temporary, so an expression-shape condition
   skipped reopening the full-expression region after freeing storage from
   the construction chain. The C++ owner reopens whenever it opened the split.
   Follow that condition directly. Eight initial matrix rows exposed the
   missing `eh_try`/`eh_end`; those rows now match exactly.
2. **Reference casts extended the conversion receiver as well as the result.**
   A cast initializer traversed into a converted prvalue receiver, recorded an
   extension path and subsequently extended the result too. Compute ownership
   before binding, bypass the path for an owned conversion temporary and
   traverse a reference cast only when its operand binds directly. Reference
   results also keep their receiver at full-expression lifetime. The runtime
   regression checks receiver destruction before the following statement and
   result destruction at scope exit, including a reference-returning converter.
3. **A throwing receiver destructor omitted a later extended result from its
   handler.** Temporary destruction used only the prefix below that receiver.
   Retain completed non-temporary actions encountered in the reverse walk and
   put them above that prefix, followed by a completed local when present.
   This reproduces the C++ owner's normal and exceptional destruction order
   without copying its whole suffix. The regression uses `noexcept(false)`
   destructor definitions, so its raw comparison enforces those exception
   regions even though normal execution does not throw (PA21 remains later).
4. **Pending-region and empty-bound-value emission differences.** Conversion
   temporaries and receiver temporaries with an empty live chain must use the
   recorded pending segment position. Constructed named objects start their
   initializer segment after their destination address; aggregate reference
   members retain the declaration's start. Local reference binding suppresses
   empty-value zeroing except for an outer direct derived-to-base binding;
   a conversion's result projection is distinct from that outer binding.
   These corrections cover aggregates, nonthrowing derived conversion results
   in allocation, conditional receivers and parenthesized casts. Broader
   matrices caught and verified the destination-address ordering too.

Four required regressions were added to the earliest owning executed lane,
`pa12/tests/controls/`:

- `500-allocation-reference-conversion-completion.t`
- `500-reference-cast-conversion-receiver-lifetime.t`
- `500-bound-empty-conversion-receiver.t`
- `500-conversion-result-pending-regions.t`

All four pass runtime/course and protected raw comparison. No fixture input,
reference, fixed manifest, baseline or protected policy was edited.

## Callable objects and surrogate indirect calls: implementation handoff

Group base: `79ed58892e0d7f477159ff059b137d16d9b0ccf9` (clean turn entry).
Stage base and last audited commit above remain unchanged. The preceding
seventh audit made implementation/evidence progress but retained six fixed
failures; its unchanged fixed result did not satisfy the next implementation
checkpoint. This group removes the callable failure rather than substituting
supplemental passing tests for fixed-manifest progress. This is a coherent
partial PA12 implementation handoff; Ralph owns the checkpoint audit and
advancement.

### Owners and behavior

Consulted frozen C++ owners:

- `sema/analyzer_calls.cpp::{call_operator,surrogate_call,indirect_call,
  resolved_call}`: member selection precedes surrogate selection; an absent,
  nonviable or ambiguous member result can use a unique surrogate. A selected
  member still gets its ordinary deletion/access checks, without retrying a
  surrogate after such a failure.
- `sema/analyzer_conversions.cpp::{visible_conversions,convert_class_operand}`
  and `sema/lookup.cpp::add_function`: own conversion declarations traverse
  newest first, followed by declaration-ordered bases, with canonical result
  hiding and imported declaration access. Surrogate ranking excludes explicit
  functions/templates, unwraps references and pointers to functions, checks
  fixed arity/variadic admission and argument sequences, and preserves the
  frozen one-pass best/ambiguity updates. Its comparison visits only the
  current candidate's fixed parameters; unused variadic slots contain default
  standard identities, not ordinary ellipsis sequences. The converted builtin
  path omits receiver viability and deletion checks just as C++ does.
- `lower/conversion_lowering.cpp::{call_conversion,converted_operand}` and
  `lower/expression_lowering.cpp::call`: the receiver conversion precedes the
  indirect call after its arguments; a function-reference result is an address,
  while a pointer-reference result gets its ordinary qualified load.
- `lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp::{begin_full_expression,creates_temporaries,
  emit_call}`, `lower/statement_lowering.cpp::class_return` and
  `lower/object_lowering.cpp::local_object`: class-return segment queries count
  the root result independently of destination materialization. A local's
  selected destination transfer does not create a source temporary. These
  distinctions preserve the scalar call spills in class returns and omit
  invented spills when copying a reference result into a local.

Rust ownership and typed flow:

`sema/callable_objects.rs` owns callable-object selection and reusable
`SurrogateScratch` vectors of complete `ArgumentSequence` values. Member
ranking runs once and passes its selected scratch range to
`procedural_calls.rs::finish_selected_call`; ordinary direct calls share that
application path. Every completion/error path clears and restores scratch.
The visible conversion inventory belongs to the analyzer's existing complete
class memo in `conversion_functions.rs`. Selection publishes one
`FunctionConversion` with `BindingId`, result `TypeId`/category and receiver
`BasePathId` on the original callee identity, preserving parenthesis aliases,
original source type/category and expression scope. Only the selected
conversion requests access, route and body demand. No surrogate declarations
or synthetic expressions are allocated.

`procedural_calls.rs::indirect_call` retains the typed indirect function
signature and ordinary argument conversions. Evaluated calls append a
`layout.rs::StorageDemand::CallBoundary(TypeId)` under their body owner.
`finish_lowering_boundaries` activates only reached owners and deduplicates
signatures before completing class passing properties/layout/reference extents.
No unused body or unevaluated call requests ABI storage. Two unit tests check
parse-once syntax marks, original callee aliases/scopes, selected-only demand,
result signatures, untouched unrelated layouts, and evaluated versus deferred
or unevaluated boundary demand.

Lowering consumes those facts in the existing per-function owners.
`lower/conversion_functions.rs` keeps function-reference results as addresses.
`lower/{temporary_scan,function}.rs` separate class-result segment presence
from materialization and recognize a local's own selected copy destination by
constructor class identity. The ordinary temporary query retains its memo;
the source-only query excludes that one destination while retaining caller-owned
parameters (including the selected copy constructor's class defaults) and nested
argument ownership. Optimizer, register allocator, code-generator algorithms,
source stack budget, dependencies and lint policy are unchanged. There is no
unsafe, fallback compiler invocation, text transport, fixture recognition,
whole-unit retry or unrelated speculative completion.

Six supplemental executed regressions are in `pa12/tests/controls/`:

```text
500-surrogate-call-selection.t
500-surrogate-reference-results.t
500-surrogate-class-result-lifetime.t
500-surrogate-class-reference-copy.t
500-surrogate-variadic-ranking.t
500-surrogate-variadic-order-ambiguous-bad.t
```

They cover member priority/nonviability/ambiguity, inherited and parenthesized
receivers, function/pointer reference results, class result/local copy and
receiver destruction, and both observable variadic declaration orders. The
rejection uses the existing control expectation mechanism and requires no
new reference sidecar. Existing fixtures, references and fixed manifest are
unchanged.

## Class declaration access context: implementation handoff

Group base: `8c79b8ac39d10960c4b50c4bc88c70d37c35f871` (clean entry).
Stage base and last audited commit above remain unchanged. The preceding
callable-object turn was **progress**: its committed group reduced fixed
failures from six to five. This group completes ordinary non-template base
clause access deferral and its expression consumers. PA12 remains partial;
Ralph owns its checkpoint/final audits and stage advancement.

### C++ owners, Rust ownership and production invariants

Consulted the frozen C++ sources directly:

- `sema/analyzer_classes.cpp::{base_classes,distinct_direct_bases,
  decltype_base_type}`: base names use the enclosing lookup scope, including
  qualified class-head scope. Failing access checks wait until all base
  edges and duplicate/base-kind validation are complete, then use the new
  class's context. Earlier bases never become lookup scopes for later ones.
- `sema/analyzer_access.cpp::{defer_access,check_access,
  settle_declaration_access,check_function_access,check_special_access}`:
  the queue retains the selected declaration, naming class, acted-on object,
  original scope and location. A protected-object violation after successful
  reachability remains immediate. Friend grants and complete base edges
  determine deferred access; lookup/overload resolution is not repeated.
- `sema/analyzer_calls.cpp::resolved_call` and
  `sema/constant_evaluator.cpp::decltype_type`: selected call/address/operator
  and special-member use checks participate in that access context. An
  unparenthesized data-member `decltype` returns the designated entity's
  declared type, including reference/cv, independently of receiver category
  or qualifications; parentheses retain category-based reference formation.

`sema/declaration_access.rs` owns the existing unit-local `Vec<Pending>` and
saved `Mark`. Base formation establishes a scoped mark for namespace, class
or block lookup, consumes only its newly appended checks in the completed
class scope, and releases them/restores the enclosing mark on every `Result`
path. Each record carries `BindingId`, naming/object `EntityId`, `ScopeId`
and `SourceOffset`. `inheritance.rs` owns base formation and its access
completion event; it preserves source order, canonical edge identity and
structural diagnostic precedence. It no longer keeps a second final-binding
list that both loses qualified naming facts and duplicates access checks.

`member_expressions.rs::AccessFailure` separates inaccessible declarations
from protected-object violations without diagnostic-string recovery. Named
expression checks and selected calls/addresses/operators use the deferred
interface. `constructor_initialization.rs`, `copy_initialization.rs` and
`construction.rs` route selected constructor/copy/destructor uses through it;
pure access/property queries used to classify special members stay pure.
`queries.rs` consumes the recorded member designation for declared `decltype`
types rather than looking up names again. These responsibilities are recorded
on the existing C++ owner rows in `source-map.tsv`.

Two Rust ownership tests check unchanged expression counts, absence of
function emission or unrelated layout demand for friend/type/call base
clauses, availability of later protected base edges, nested class context,
and release/restoration of access state after success and rejection. The
three new executed regressions belong to PA11's access/object-model lane:

```text
pa11/tests/controls/base-clause-declaration-access.t
pa11/tests/controls/base-clause-decltype-access.t
pa11/tests/controls/base-clause-friend-access-does-not-escape-bad.t
```

They exercise namespace/nested/local friend bases, alias and unevaluated call
bases, selected overloaded addresses/operators, member `decltype`, special
member access and isolation of a later unrelated declaration. Multiple-base
ordering, duplicate precedence and protected-object cases remain additional
scratch probes, outside PA11's single-inheritance fixture contract. Existing
fixtures, references, manifest, oracle sources and protected checks are
unchanged. No dependency, unsafe code, implementation fallback, text transport,
new optimization or environment/process access is introduced. Lowering,
optimizer, register allocation and per-function backend ownership are unchanged.

## Abort runtime boundary and singleton roles: implementation handoff

Group base: `480bccddb77cdf897602f7c861b2022ea0f55a46` (clean entry).

### Earliest divergence, C++ owners and typed ownership

The static-reference fixture's raw LowIR comparison isolated its only difference
to the `__builtin_abort` declaration. Complete temporary backing storage,
constructed flags, reference addresses, conditional construction and reverse
shutdown actions already matched. No lifetime algorithm or generated instruction
sequence was changed to fix that case.

Consulted the frozen C++ owners directly:

- `lower/symbol_lowering.cpp::{function_object,function_metadata,function_signature}`:
  abort's object is `abort`; course LowIR gives it `role=terminate`, without
  C linkage, and forces `unwind=no, return=noreturn` independently of the
  source declaration's exception specification. Existing builtin identity
  remains the semantic selection fact.
- `lower/program_lowering.cpp::{resolve_singleton_roles,terminate_function}`:
  scalar allocation functions and the demanded `std::terminate` runtime are
  primary owners; array forms and abort are secondary. Ranking prefers a
  definition, then a primary owner, retaining first-demand order on ties.
  Ordinary pending nonowner declarations become internal forwarders. Already
  published runtime declarations participate in ranking but do not become
  forwarding bodies. This distinction also preserves the frozen behavior
  when a source explicitly defines the reserved abort builtin.
- `lower/function_lowering.cpp::lower_forwarder`: the original boundary and
  non-role metadata survive; role and object identity are cleared on the
  forwarding body, which passes positional arguments and returns its call.
- `lower/expression_lowering.cpp::{bind_converted,extension_path,persistent_alive}`
  and `lower/global_lowering.cpp::{static_temporary,static_temporary_constructed,
  define_reference}` confirmed that the existing lifetime path was already
  translated for this fixture; it stays unchanged.

Rust `lower/symbols.rs` consumes `BuiltinFunction::Abort` for object/role metadata.
`lower/function.rs::builtin_boundary` shares forced boundary facts between
ordinary declarations and definitions without transferring them to an indirect
function type. `SymbolTable` retains only demanded `SymbolId`, `Role` and
`runtime_roles::Origin::{Primary,Secondary,Runtime}` records across source units.
The former allocation-only owner, `allocation_runtime.rs`, is now
`runtime_roles.rs`; the same algorithm serves allocation and terminate roles.
`lower/exception_helpers.rs` keeps its existing demanded helper identities;
`symbols.rs::terminate_helper` registers the runtime primary once.

At unit completion the resolver visits those demanded records, reads typed
LowIR signatures and builds each needed forwarder with its own `FunctionBuilder`.
The builder streams/releases its body before the next one. The program keeps
symbols, boundaries and serialized emission spans; no semantic owner crosses
that boundary. Existing source parsing, selected calls, static-reference storage,
optimizer, register allocator and code-generation algorithms are unchanged.
There is no new dependency, unsafe code, compiler subprocess, source recognition,
textual phase transport or speculative body demand. Object-mode host abort
metadata remains a later native-backend boundary; PA12's due course mode is exact.
`source-map.tsv` records the three translated lowering responsibilities.

The new driver ownership test checks coalesced cross-unit identity in both
helper demand orders, one ordinary terminate role, forced boundaries, released
forwarding bodies, and an unused inline memory builtin that stays undemanded.
Two executed supplemental regressions live in their earliest owning lanes:

plus audit fixes in `6a8608a5c298de61dd557a305caf60d12c50cdb4`.

### Scope, C++ owners and architecture review

Read `AGENTS.md`, `CLONE_CONTRACT.md`, `spec.md`, the PA12 handout, stage ledger,
Rust conversion plan and source map. Consulted these frozen owners directly:

- `sema/analyzer_calls.cpp::{call_operator,surrogate_call,resolved_call,indirect_call}`
  and `sema/analyzer_conversions.cpp::{conversion_functions,visible_conversions,
  convert_class_operand}`: member-first selection, fallback on nonviability or
  ambiguity, visible declaration order/hiding, variadic default sequences,
  selected argument conversion and the receiver's recorded conversion.
- `sema/analyzer_classes.cpp::{base_classes,decltype_base_type}`,
  `sema/analyzer_access.cpp::{check_access,defer_access,settle_declaration_access}`
  and `sema/constant_evaluator.cpp::decltype_type`: complete base-list access
  context, immediate protected-object failures, original diagnostic locations,
  declared member types versus parenthesized value categories.
- `lower/symbol_lowering.cpp::{function_object,function_metadata,function_signature}`,
  `lower/program_lowering.cpp::resolve_singleton_roles` and
  `lower/function_lowering.cpp::{lower_forwarder,finish_body}`: forced abort
  boundary, primary/secondary/published runtime identity, definition preference,
  demand-order ties, forwarding metadata and final handler emission.
- `lower/conversion_lowering.cpp::{conversion_object,call_conversion}`,
  `lower/object_lowering.cpp::{temporary,destroy_local}` and
  `lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp::{temporary_done,temporary_alive,region_event,
  destroy_temporaries,close_region,place_blocks}`: receiver construction,
  empty-region splits, normal/exceptional cleanup and explicit-region ownership.

The reviewed Rust semantic owners are `sema/{callable_objects,procedural_calls,
conversion_functions,declaration_access,inheritance,member_expressions,queries,
layout,function_resolution,construction,constructor_initialization,
copy_initialization}.rs`. Lowering owners are `lower/{conversion_functions,
function,temporary_scan,runtime_roles,symbols,lifetime,lifetime_regions,
element_temporaries}.rs`; the driver ownership regression is in `driver/ast.rs`.
The source map retains their C++ responsibilities and records the audit fixes.

Callable selection consumes the existing original AST, canonical class/function
TypeIds and BindingIds. The visible conversion cache is complete-class-owned;
its explicit path stack preserves declaration order, hiding and repeated-base
identity. Ranking reuses the existing typed argument-sequence engine and restores
scratch on every Result path. The selected conversion retains the original
receiver facts, parenthesis aliases, scope and physical base path; lowering
never searches the class or reruns overload selection. Only the selected use
adds function demand. The indirect signature's queued storage demand is attached
to its body owner; reached-owner filtering and TypeId deduplication precede ABI
preparation. Unevaluated/unused bodies do not force layouts or class passing facts.

Declaration access records only failed reachability checks under its saved mark,
with binding, naming/object identity, original scope and location. Structural base
validation precedes settlement in the complete derived context; the mark's suffix
is released and its enclosing context restored on success and failure. A reached
protected-object violation remains immediate. Unparenthesized member decltype
reads the selected entity's declared reference/cv type; parentheses use category.
No synthetic expression or duplicate parser was introduced.

Abort metadata consumes the typed builtin identity and shares forced no-unwind,
noreturn facts between declarations and definitions. Indirect function types
retain their own boundaries. Runtime-role records contain only demanded SymbolIds,
roles and origin, with definition then primary ranking and stable demand-order
ties. Each forwarder has one function-owned builder and released body; signatures
and serialized output spans outlive the frontend without retaining semantic owners.
The runtime scan is bounded by demanded role records, rather than all declarations.
Cross-unit demand order, explicit builtin definitions and published-runtime cases
were checked against raw C++ output.

No fixture recognition, C++ implementation delegation, environment/process access,
unsafe code, duplicate parser, blanket lint suppression, mechanical module split,
new dependency, whole-unit retry or unrelated layout/body scan was found in this
range. Canonical IDs remain lookup keys. Target numeric ranking boundaries,
diagnostic bytes, object extents, class passing, role metadata, call-result spills
and cleanup/emission order were compared without normalization beyond the
protected contract. PA12 still uses the permitted frozen backend as an evaluator.
The optimizer, register allocator and native code-generation algorithms are unchanged.

### Findings and fixes

1. **Lost region after a trivial surrogate receiver.** `S()(1)` returning a
   class with a nontrivial destructor closed the receiver's empty region, then
   omitted the result's destruction region. The initial independent matrix
   found two raw LowIR mismatches despite successful compiler exits. The fix
   retains a function-owned temporary split position, distinct from allocation
   storage splits. Ordinary destruction can reopen at that split only with live
   cleanups. Explicit guards, unwinding and tracked bodies retain their own
   regions. Positions are rebased when an empty marker is removed, saved and
   restored across explicit element-temporary scopes, and cleared at expression end.
2. **Unpublished trailing handlers.** A throwing receiver destructor could close
   an empty result region whose cleanup handler had already been allocated. Rust
   left the handler in the pending queue at function end. `lower/function.rs`
   now publishes ordinary pending chains before lexical and late chains, matching
   C++ `finish_body`; it does not invent or reconstruct a handler.
3. **Receiver value-initialization lost under a class return.** The converter's
   general return-initialization policy suppressed `zeroinit` on an empty surrogate
   receiver while the indirect call forwarded its class result. The original
   builtin-operand fact now distinguishes that receiver from the ordinary
   conversion initializing the return destination. Its own initialization survives.

Executed regressions are in the earliest owning PA12 controls lane:

```text
500-surrogate-trivial-receiver-result-cleanup.t
500-surrogate-trailing-cleanup-handlers.t
500-surrogate-return-receiver-value-init.t
```

They check destruction order/counts and forwarding behavior at runtime. Their
protected course receipts also require exact emitted bytes, including the otherwise
unreferenced handler and empty-class zeroing. Existing fixtures were not reformatted.

Development evidence records the initially overbroad destructor event, which
opened regions in handler bodies and regressed three accepted PA12 identities.
It was corrected to an ordinary-destruction event before final validation; the
final fixed-manifest identity comparison has zero losses. Findings were fixed
even though the fixed count remains 358: these are additional supported inputs,
not a claim of new fixed-manifest implementation progress.

## Class-result exception queries and destructor completion handoff

Implementation base: **4c8f1dabd87173ced13d86290b4502c06f277706**. Stage base
remains **b9f3dba87a8cc2859ff489ed09f1ddf2cb95dc4f**; last independently audited
implementation remains **6a8608a5c298de61dd557a305caf60d12c50cdb4**. This handoff
records implementation evidence; it does not advance that audit anchor or Ralph's
stage. The preceding turn is **progress**: the committed eighth audit corrected
receiver/result boundaries and supplied the three concrete residual traces used
here. The authoritative turn-start checkout was clean at the implementation base.

Evidence root: `/home/vishvananda/work/private/v5rust/artifacts/pa12-exception-completion-20261015`.
Generated probes, raw comparison receipts, measurements and logs stay outside git.

### Completed behavior group and source review

All three remaining fixed failures first diverged in semantic analysis, owned by
frozen `sema/analyzer_exceptions.cpp`, `analyzer_construction.cpp` and
`analyzer_declarations.cpp`. Consulted directly in oracle revision
`3438f5aea24d8fcc9a0113316f7e27c890cd5ac6`, together with
`analyzer_exception_sets.cpp`, `analyzer_classes.cpp`,
`lower/function_lowering.cpp` and `lower/cleanup_lowering.cpp`:

- `temporary_may_throw` and `may_throw`: a class-valued call, conditional or
  projection can have no construction plan on the result. Its destructor still
  contributes; the selected call and source operands supply their own effects.
  Rust `sema/throw_queries.rs` consumes the existing original `ExprId` facts and
  worklist, retaining constructed/elided-copy/default-argument handling. It no
  longer substitutes missing-implementation status 86 for these ordinary results.
- `declare_destructor` and `implicit_destructor_nothrow`: an explicit specification
  stays owned by its declaration and is not evaluated merely to finish its class.
  `sema/destruction.rs` now stores `DestructorUnwind::{Implicit { nothrow },
  Declared(EntityId)}`. The existing declaration-owned
  `ExceptionState::{Pending,Evaluating,Value}` remains the resolution memo.
  Nested explicit specifications can therefore inspect the completed outer class
  when actually queried. Implicit destructors still inspect only their recorded
  base/member edges, ignoring reference referents and flattening array types.
- `default_definition` and `destructor_deleted`: defaulting after the first
  declaration checks the implicit definition's deletedness before storage/body
  preparation. A private, deleted or inaccessible subobject destructor, or a
  union's nontrivial variant, rejects at the definition's original location.
  `SubobjectDestruction` shares inherited triviality/deletedness between ordinary
  class completion and this check without manufacturing exception facts.
  User-provided triviality and first-declaration exception facts are retained.
- `redeclare_function`: an omitted destructor specification infers subobject
  unwind in semantic modes. `sema/exceptions.rs` records an
  `ImplicitDestructor` comparison, which compares nonthrowing compatibility
  without conflating the implicit set with a written dynamic set. Types mode
  preserves its existing frozen behavior. Lookup limits are restored on error.
- Valid out-of-class defaulted definitions contribute typed function boundary
  roots through `sema/special_members.rs`, so layouts exist for emitted members.
  Unused inline definitions retain demand-only layout and emission. No global
  declaration scan or additional initialization/body analysis was introduced.
- `lower_definition`'s inert-destructor early return retains its guard marker
  without placing a landing or demanding terminate helpers. Rust
  `lower/{function,lifetime_regions}.rs` follows that return using the existing
  destructor-inert memo. This also preserves a reproducible oracle defect
  documented below, rather than repairing the C++ output.

`sema/construction.rs` owns the after-first defaulted-definition check;
`sema/lowering.rs` remains the typed output interface. The existing destructor
property regression was adapted to read declared exception facts. The two new
Rust tests prove unchanged syntax-node counts, retained call targets without an
invented constructor, no output demand/layout for unevaluated class results,
one reused nested specification, an unrelated pending specification left alone,
and no layout demand for an unused inline defaulted destructor.

The source map records these responsibilities. Compact `EntityId`, `BindingId`,
`TypeId` and original expression identities remain the phase boundaries. No
second parser, syntax clone, name-based lowering recovery, whole-program retry,
unsafe code, dependency, process/environment access or fallback was introduced.
Lowering's guard state remains function-owned. Optimizer, allocator and native
code-generation algorithms are unchanged.

### Frozen-oracle defect and next boundary

`oracle-defect/inert-defaulted.cpp` is a standalone reproducer:

```cpp
struct Member { ~Member() noexcept(false) {} };
struct Owner { Member member; ~Owner() noexcept(sizeof(Owner)>0); };
Owner::~Owner() noexcept(sizeof(Owner)>0) = default;
int main() { return 0; }
```

Both frontend tools exit 0 with byte-identical LowIR. Each destructor entry
retains `eh_try ^function_exception_landing_1` but emits no such block because
`lower_definition` returns after the inert-destructor check, before
`place_terminate_landing`. Frozen `lowir2native -O0` rejects it with
`undefined or invalid block target` at LowIR line 5. Reproduce using the command
arguments in `oracle-defect/summary.json`; exact frontend receipts and backend
stderr are retained there. A proposed new runtime control was removed before
commit because the oracle itself fails it; its generated source remains a probe.
No existing control or acceptance policy was removed or weakened. Independent
port work and all due validation completed; the malformed oracle bytes remain
exact, pending an independently authorized baseline decision.

There are **no remaining failing fixed PA12 case IDs**. Class-result exception
queries and complete-class/defaulted-destructor completion are implemented as
one coherent group, including the related production invariants exposed by the
matrix. The next boundary is Ralph's independent full-stage checkpoint/final
architecture audit and advancement decision, not PA13 implementation within
this handoff. The ledger does not certify every C++ owner complete from fixture
counts. Later-stage dependencies remain outside PA12's handout; PA34 still
requires the full frozen-source object/tool equality proof and PA35 is inactive.

### Responsibility and architecture review

Read the contract, specification, complete PA12 handout, conversion plan,
source map and stage ledger. The review combines the preceding independent
responsibility audits with renewed inspection of the latest exception-completion
implementation and the remaining observable boundaries. `coverage.json` maps
every handout behavior group to C++/Rust owners and evidence;
`stage-ownership.json` retains all **43** PA12 source-map rows and verifies their
Rust owners exist. This is PA12 responsibility coverage, not PA33's later
whole-implementation census.

Directly consulted frozen C++ owners include `analyzer_copy.cpp`,
`analyzer_construction.cpp`, `analyzer_initialization.cpp`,
`analyzer_declarations.cpp`, `analyzer_exceptions.cpp`,
`analyzer_exception_sets.cpp` and their call/conversion/class consumers;
`lower/{copy,function,object,cleanup,tracked_lifetime,condition,allocation,
expression,lexical_cleanup}_lowering.cpp`, symbol lowering and definition order.
Compared constructor candidate enumeration, direct/copy-list contexts,
defaulted/deleted availability, class-result/destructor exception effects,
complete-class scheduling, declaration-object context, target count arithmetic,
cleanup chain keys, guard completion and serialized instruction/block/helper order.

The cumulative completed responsibilities cover copy/move construction and
assignment, direct/indirect class parameters/results and extents/noalias facts,
named return slots, demand-only helpers, trivial storage prefixes, bit-field
allocation units and base tail padding, delegation and later definitions,
normal/exceptional temporary lifetimes and shared cleanup suffixes, ref-qualified
calls, conversion operators/surrogates, unions, scalar/array explicit storage,
cookies and checked count/list-prefix boundaries. Fresh probes vary acceptance
and rejection, cv/access, list nesting, 0/1/2/8/9 array boundaries, negative and
64-bit limit counts, destructor specifications and branch/arrow lifetimes.
Retained independent multi-unit, conversion, allocation, union, delegation and
value-path probes were replayed against the final compiler.

The production path is `dev/bin/cppgm.rs` -> `driver/ast.rs` -> one `SyntaxUnit`
-> unit-owned semantic `Graph` -> typed LowIR lowering. It invokes no C++ phase.
The frozen backend remains solely the handout-authorized evaluator for controls.
The source buffers, AST/list stores, canonical TypeIds, ExprIds, EntityIds,
BindingIds and ConstructorIds retain their original owners. Parser checkpoints
are bounded LIFO marks; no new parser, syntax cloning or textual phase transport
was introduced. Emission follows reached-owner demand edges, not a declaration
scan. Temporary summary caches use immutable-graph ExprIds with separate
root/operand queries; constructor properties, declarations and bodies have
separate unit-owned memos. No unrelated layout/body is demanded by ranking.
Function builders, cleanup chains, scan scratch and lifetime flags die with their
function; tool-output serialization is not reparsed as production transport.

Array count widening, PTRDIFF_MAX/cookie subtraction, target wrap, prefix tests,
small/loop thresholds and subobject storage spans were checked against their
C++ owners. Ordering uses source/demand identities and explicit entry ranks.
The source audit and strict Clippy enforce file/function/nesting limits, safe
code and environment/process placement. No dependency, blanket lint allowance,
mechanical split, fixture tailoring, unsafe block or fallback was added.
Optimizer, allocator and native code-generation policies are unchanged.

Missing-path review distinguishes later handout exclusions from required work:
virtual dispatch is PA13; template-aware values and constexpr interpretation
belong to later assignments; local-static class/reference guards are explicitly
PA16 in their C++ owner; throwing/catching and member pointers remain PA21/PA22.
The due semantic specification path now uses the normal typed expression engine;
its earlier types-only helper remains for preceding modes. Guards on impossible
lowering shapes are not treated as successful C++ rejections. No PA12-owned
unfinished path was retained as an acceptance exception.

### Findings resolved before acceptance

1. Generic list/constructor ranking omitted implicit copies. Add a property-owned
   callable BindingId memo, independent of the body-plan memo, and enumerate it
   only for eligible construction contexts. Selected list arguments keep that
   identity through initialization. Functional aggregate casts first check
   constructor viability; ordinary direct initialization retains its own failure
   diagnostic. The ownership regression proves repeated declaration queries reuse
   IDs without building bodies, demanding storage or changing syntax.
2. Semantic `noexcept` specifications used a restricted earlier-stage operand
   typer, returning status 86 for valid contextual class conversions. They now
   retain normal expression/conversion facts under suppressed output demand and
   evaluate the selected nonconstexpr call as an ordinary rejection. Scoped
   declaration context supplies the member's qualified `this`, restoring the
   enclosing body/default/member context on semantic failure. Ordinary semantic
   arithmetic does not read the types-mode-only conversion table.
3. Synthesized copy entries completed function guards after C++ had returned from
   `finish_body`. Preserve the frozen early-return bytes. Counted array-copy
   cleanup still terminates through its existing function guard, matching
   `emit_resume`; this is separate from final guard placement.
4. Branch-temporary hidden regions and guarded overloaded-arrow results had
   incomplete ownership. A bounded reusable branch scan selects the existing
   hidden segment; arrow results use its tracked/guard owner. Destruction below
   that segment uses ordinary unwind chains, while `finish_tracking` owns its
   separate conditional chain. Eager argument regions open at the pending
   full-expression position, including scalar work before the eventual call.

Five regressions are in the earliest owning executed PA12 controls lane:
`500-implicit-copy-braced-arguments.t`,
`500-aggregate-copy-braced-argument-bad.t`,
`500-noexcept-class-boolean-nonconstant-bad.t`,
`500-noexcept-declaration-object-context.t`, and
`500-guarded-arrow-result-temporaries.t`.
They pass their course outcomes and protected byte comparisons. Development logs
retain the initially missed combined-list region, tracked throwing-destructor
suffix and incomplete arithmetic fact; all were fixed before the final runs.

The historical explicitly-noexcept defaulted-copy discrepancy is now closed as
an exact-clone discrepancy. Four standalone copy/move probes, with scalar or
nine-element class subobjects, produce identical frontend bytes. Both retain the
frozen missing landing block and the frozen `lowir` validator rejects each with
`undefined or invalid block target`; `oracle-defects/summary.json` records exact
commands/exits and raw diagnostics. This supersedes earlier ledger statements
that Rust still differed. These invalid oracle outputs, the previously recorded
inert-destructor case and reference-switch case are reported baseline defects,
not valid-LowIR/runtime successes. No reference or baseline was repaired, and no
required course input fails because of them.

The 2,048-parenthesis class-argument exception query reaches Rust's genuine guard
at **8 MiB**. The protected receipt has `match: true` and
`stack_qualification.passed: true`: at the shared **16 MiB** stack both tools
exit **0**, with identical streams and nonempty LowIR SHA-256
`0c5223991891f4c4ac3e88459e26158fa5ee6770e54cdc8572f7a313d7d03ad5`.
See `resources/stack-receipt.json` and its retained raw qualification directory.
The original caller-visible guard result remains intact. No native exhaustion
cutoff, diagnostic position or frame size was calibrated; the byte-budget guard
is unchanged.

### Acceptance boundary

PA12's full due stage is accepted on responsibility review, current architecture
and exact output/failure evidence, not fixture count alone. There are **no failing
fixed case IDs, course invocations or outstanding PA12 audit findings**. The next
dependency boundary is PA13 polymorphism, subject to Ralph's stage advancement.
PA33 release/census and PA34 frozen-source object/tool fixed-point proof remain
later gates; PA35 is inactive. This audit completes the existing PA12 phase goal
without creating/resetting a goal or editing Ralph state.
